After EA, Silver Lake and PIF Reportedly Want Unity

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Contributor at DualShockers, former Esports Insider DACH content lead, former Escapist Magazine Germany content lead, software engineering background.

Unity Software has held talks to be acquired by a consortium that includes private equity firm Silver Lake and Saudi Arabia’s Public Investment Fund, according to Reuters. The group discussed a valuation of around $25 billion, whilst Unity’s market cap sits at roughly $21.5 billion after its latest earnings report. Shares rose about 4% in extended trading once the story broke.

Nothing is certain. The approach came in recent weeks, and Unity has reportedly been working with an investment bank to weigh its options ever since. Reuters notes that the plan and terms could still change, and Unity declined to comment.

Silver Lake and PIF have done this before. Both were part of the consortium behind the $55 billion EA take-private that closed in August, which ranks among the largest leveraged buyouts ever. A Unity deal would be their second enormous gaming bet in a matter of months.

Silver Lake isn’t walking in cold, either. It invested up to $400 million in Unity back in 2017 and still owns around 8% of the company. Its Egon Durban sits on Unity’s board, a dual role that would normally see him recuse himself from any board discussion of a sale. That’s the kind of detail that looks tidy on paper and gets picked at later.

Most people know Unity as the engine behind thousands of mobile, console and PC games, but its reach goes further than that. It’s used in automotive design, filmmaking, architecture and industrial simulation too. The company started out in Denmark in 2004, went public in September 2020, and has since wandered into advertising technology.

That advertising side is currently pulling its weight. Unity’s third quarter beat estimates, with revenue up 32% to $622.1 million and adjusted earnings of 44 cents per share. Strong growth in its AI-powered Vector platform helped it post its first quarterly profit on a GAAP basis since the IPO, alongside a solid showing from its game-development business. The takeover report landed straight afterwards. A company that has just turned its first profit is a much easier sell to a buyer, and I doubt that’s a coincidence.

There’s a practical angle too. According to Reuters, Unity’s tools are already used by Scopely, the developer behind Monopoly Go!, and Niantic, the creator of Pokémon Go, both of which are owned by a group that includes PIF, Silver Lake and Affinity Partners. The buyers would be acquiring something their own studios already rely on.

PIF’s route into gaming mostly runs through Savvy Games Group, which has had a mandate to invest $38 billion in the sector since 2022. Savvy bought Scopely for $4.9 billion in 2023, and Scopely then bought Niantic’s games business, Pokémon Go included, for $3.5 billion. ESL and FACEIT sit under the same umbrella. In January, PIF moved around $12 billion in listed gaming shares across to Savvy, including its stakes in Nintendo and Bandai Namco, followed by its roughly $3 billion Take-Two stake a month later. Add the EA deal on top and the list of major publishers with no Saudi money attached gets shorter every year.

The group discussed a valuation of around $25 billion, whilst Unity’s market cap sits at roughly $21.5 billion after its latest earnings report.

Savvy’s chief, Brian Ward, said at GDC that it doesn’t plan to use those stakes to push publishers into big changes. Taking him at his word, a minority shareholder is one thing. Owning the engine that other studios build on, including studios that compete with your own, is a different kind of position entirely.

Unity has been here before with developer trust. In September 2023 it announced a Runtime Fee that charged studios per install, and the backlash was fierce enough that CEO John Riccitiello stepped down within weeks. The fee was scrapped completely in September 2024, and current CEO Matthew Bromberg later admitted Unity had been “practically at war with our customers”. A leveraged buyout means debt, and debt has to be paid from somewhere. Developers are going to want to know where.

Right now, none of that is more than speculation. There are talks, an investment bank, and a number somewhere north of where the stock was trading. Studios will be watching to see which of those three moves first.

Dropbox acquires Tigris Data for $350M, releases new AI productivity features

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Dropbox Inc. is acquiring cloud storage startup Tigris Data Inc. for $350 million.

The company announced the deal today in conjunction with a major update to its product portfolio. It introduced DBX Agent Cloud, a service that artificial intelligence agents can use to store their data. Dropbox is also adding new AI productivity features to its flagship file sharing platform.

Sunnyvale, California-based Tigris Data offers an object storage service that runs across multiple public clouds. The company says that it enables applications to read and write several times faster than competing offerings. Furthermore, the service includes several features designed to optimize AI workloads.

AI agents often run in sandboxes that include, among other resources, the data they need to carry out their work. Loading data into a sandbox can be a relatively time-consuming process, which slows agent performance. Tigris Data offers a feature called bucket forms that speeds up the workflow. Furthermore, it prevents agents from modifying information that is used by other workloads.

Tigris Data’s service monitors the locations from which agents access records. When it detects a new location, it moves a copy of the records to a nearby server in order to reduce latency. The service caches the most frequently used files on high-speed storage infrastructure to further reduce access times.

Dropbox stated that Tigris Data will support its push into the AI infrastructure market. The company debuted its first entry into that product category, DBX Agent Cloud, in conjunction with the acquisition announcement. Like Tigris Data’s service, it’s an S3-compatible object storage offering optimized for AI agents.

Dropbox plans to add a file system capability further down the line. Object storage is best suited for unstructured datasets, while file systems are used to store records that are organized into folders. Dropbox plans to release the technology alongside “content processing capabilities.” The company says that those features will make it easier for AI models to process information kept in DBX Agent Cloud.

The offering is rolling out alongside a major overhaul of Dropbox’s flagship file sharing platform. An enhanced search bar will enable users to look for images by entering a description of the file they seek. Furthermore, Dropbox can now respond to questions about a file by generating a natural language summary. A worker could, for example, request an overview of the changes that colleagues have made to an important folder over the past week.

Dropbox can not only edit questions about files but also edit them. The company says that users can have the built-in AI models change a file’s format, move it to a different folder or combine it with other records into a PDF document. Several other actions are supported as well. Users can mix and match actions into multistep workflows.

The new AI productivity features are initially available to customers in the US, UK, and Australia with Standard and Advanced subscriptions. Dropbox plans to expand access next year.

Democratic socialist defeats incumbent Karen Bass in Los Angeles mayor's race

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Nithya Raman, once seen as a long-shot candidate, will be LA's new mayor

Democratic socialist Nithya Raman has toppled incumbent Karen Bass in the race for mayor of Los Angeles.

Raman's win stunned political watchers in California after she entered the race as a long-shot candidate against Bass, a political heavyweight who previously served in Congress and had never lost a race for political office.

Raman has frequently been compared to New York City Mayor Zohran Mamdani. Both are democratic socialists and ran on progressive platforms.

Her win solidifies that America's two biggest cities will now be led by democratic socialists, which include some of the most high-profile politicians in the US, such as Mamdani and Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez.

Los Angeles was always likely to have a Democratic mayor but the question in the city - the second most populous city in the US - was just how left voters would be willing to go.

Bass, also Democrat, announced that she had called and conceded to Raman in a post on social media on Thursday, writing "while this is not the outcome we hoped for, the people of Los Angeles have spoken."

"Moving this city forward is what Los Angeles needs from all of us," Bass wrote. "I love Los Angeles. I believe in Los Angeles. And I will continue to ensure the promise of a better life belongs to all of us."

Raman, a member of city council and Indian immigrant, ran on a platform of change and fixing the "broken status quo and the politicians who failed them over and over again".

She ran on cutting homelessness, increasing housing in the city, saving Hollywood jobs from continued cuts and protecting residents from immigration enforcement efforts. Raman also vowed to fix the city's many issues, including helping beautification efforts, fixing roads and even helping bring back nightlife in the city.

The major issue in the race, however, was the LA fires, which killed dozens of people and razed neighbourhoods across the area in 2025. Bass faced intense political fallout over her absence during the emergency and the city's response to the disaster.

The next mayor in LA will oversee hosting the Super Bowl in 2027 and the 2028 Summer Olympics.

Watch: Mamdani says he's a democratic socialist. What does that mean?

Raman entered the race as a long-shot candidate but slowly shored up support, especially from younger voters, before besting reality star Spencer Pratt in the primary race earlier this year.

Raman's win over Bass has all the appearances of echoing voter sentiment across the US as part of sweeping Democratic wins, including in deep-Republican country that hadn't seen liberal leaders in decades.

Washington DC also elected democratic socialist Janeese Lewis George as its new mayor, furthering progressive leadership in another major US city.

US President Donald Trump was asked at the White House about the wins by politicians on the left flank of the Democratic Party and said, "If you go by history, it's going to be a disaster".

Such candidates have become a focal point of attacks by Republicans.

Bass had been a prominent member of Congress before becoming LA's first black female mayor. Her roots are closely tied to LA and its history, even working as a community organiser during the violent 1992 riots over police brutality in the beating of Rodney King, who was black.

Salon.com Shuts Down Cultural and Political Site After 31 Years

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Salon.com, the website that became popular in the early days of the internet offering cultural commentary, progressive political analysis, lifestyle content and more, is shutting down, the company announced Thursday.

“When Salon launched in November 1995, the web was a very different place. A small group of journalists set out to create something original: a publication with a strong voice, a curiosity about the world and a belief that the internet could bring journalism and readers together in new ways. They helped pioneer a form of journalism that would come to define an entirely new era of publishing,” the editorial team wrote in a statement. Related Stories Netflix Is Profitable and Still Growing. Why Did It Just Make a Huge Round of Layoffs and Oust Its Top Content Exec? Netflix, ‘Elite’ Star Ester Expósito and Locarno Winner Mar Coll Adapt ‘La Regenta,’ a ‘Fresh Female Perspective’ on a Big Spanish Classic (EXCLUSIVE)

The San Francisco-based site was known for launching numerous writers’ careers and was a daily stop for many readers in the days before social media. Among the film critics that wrote for Salon were Stephanie Zacharek, Andrew O’Hehir and Charles Taylor. The site’s television writers and critics included Heather Havrilesky, Hanh Nguyen, Maureen Ryan and Melanie McFarland.

Salon joins later-arriving sites such as Buzzfeed and Gawker that found it difficult to monetize cultural and topical reporting for a general audience and eventually shut down.

“The business realities of independent digital journalism have changed profoundly over the years,” the editors continued in their statement. “After careful consideration, we have reached the difficult decision that our publishing model is no longer sustainable. We know this news will be deeply disappointing to many of you, and we share that sadness.

“Thirty-one years is a remarkable run, but what matters most is what happened during those years: the journalism, the ideas, the voices, the conversations and the community that grew around them. Salon helped demonstrate what digital journalism could be and could become. We hope that legacy will continue to matter long after today.

“Thank you for being part of our story. It has been an extraordinary privilege,” the statement concluded.

WhatsApp’s AI-powered photo expansion tool starts rolling out on iOS

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Select WhatsApp users on TestFlight and in the main app are now seeing the option to expand an image in the app’s photo editor. Here are the details.

iPhone users with access to Apple Intelligence have a native AI-powered photo expansion tool called Extend, which lets users pinch or drag to expand an image beyond its original borders.

This feature, which is also present on AI chatbots such as ChatGPT and image-editing platforms like Photoshop, is now also making its way into WhatsApp.

According to WABetaInfo, the platform is rolling out a Meta AI-powered image expansion tool, though it is currently limited to select users, primarily those in WhatsApp’s beta program via TestFlight.

When users select a photo in their chats, WhatsApp opens the drawing editor. Here, they can choose to apply some AI effect to improve it. Among these, users can find a new option to expand their photos. Once users select this option, Meta AI takes a few seconds to analyze the photo and generate the expanded version. Once the process is complete, WhatsApp shows the expanded photo in the drawing editor, and users can choose to send it. Note that this feature is also compatible with status updates.

The site notes that if the user isn’t happy with the result, they can tap an undo button to revert to the original and then try using the tool again to generate a different result.

As is usually the case with beta features on WhatsApp, there is no telling when it might become widely available.

That said, given that it has also rolled out to users in the Android beta and some users on the public iOS version already have access, a broader rollout might not be too far off.

These Panera Snowman Cups Are Only Available for a Day—Here’s How to Get One

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Panera Snowman Cup is giving the Starbucks Bearista Cup some cozy competition. Here's what you need to know about the limited-edition mystery boxes.

I don’t generally need an incentive to stop at Panera in December. Any of those delicious soups in a bread bowl is a fairly convincing argument on its own. But this year, the chain is giving us another reason to visit: collectible glass snowman cups, with a chance to get one free.

If you’ve been admiring the Starbucks Bearista cup, these little snowmen might also deserve a spot on your wish list. Panera’s version comes in a mystery box containing one of two designs, naughty or nice. The free cup offer runs on Dec. 3, and with only 12 available at each participating cafe, there are a few details to keep in mind before you go.

The limited-edition glass cups come in two designs inspired by the naughty-and-nice theme of Panera’s holiday packaging. Each mystery box holds one snowman cup, so you won’t know which you’ve received until you open it.

There are also a few new things to drink while you admire your cute cup. Panera’s new holiday menu, rolling out this week, includes peppermint white mocha and pistachio white mocha beverages. Both flavors are available as a latte, iced coffee or Frozen Java, finished with peppermint bark pieces or pistachio pieces. The iced coffees have sweet cream cold foam, and the lattes and Frozen Java drinks get whipped cream.

On Dec. 3, the first 12 customers at each participating U.S. cafe who make a qualifying purchase of $25 or more will receive a free Snowman Cup, while supplies last. There’s a limit of one per person, so you may want to make that Panera outing a breakfast date.

You’ll need to order inside the cafe at the cashier. Kiosk, Rapid Pick-Up, drive-thru, catering and delivery orders—including those placed through third-party sites—don’t qualify.

If you don’t get your hands on one of those first 12 boxes, you’ll have another way to find a snowman. A limited number of cups will also be available through the Panera Shop for $22 each. An online sale date hasn’t been announced yet, so keep an eye on the shop if you’re hoping to grab one.

Threat actor targets Indian fintechs on weekends when defenders aren’t watching

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

CERT-IN warns of ‘significant escalation’ in AI-powered attacks, some after victims tried to improve defences

India’s Computer Emergency Response Team has warned the nation’s financial services industry of a “significant escalation” in attacks by what it describes as an “AI-assisted financial threat actor” that has re-targeted victims after they flubbed remediation efforts.

CERT-IN first detected the attacks in October and issued advisory in which it warned digital lending platforms, fintech entities, payment service providers, and wallet operators that attackers were targeting internet-facing applications, exposed APIs and privileged endpoints. If the attackers found a way in, they tried to use payment APIs to steal money.

On Thursday, the CERT published an update in which it said it continues to hear of successful attacks, including from outfits that tried to remediate their systems after earlier incidents.

“The same entities have been deliberately re-targeted, including after incomplete remediation and attacks have been timed to coincide with holidays, weekends and other low-monitoring windows to delay detection and response,” the CERT warned.

CERT-IN has decided the attackers need a name and chose “CRAFTY FALCON.”

The security org has also revealed that the crew uses AI-enhanced tools including the ARTEX pen-testing tool, the Cairn attack search engine, and the CyberStrikeAI framework – all of which have been linked to users who appear to speak Chinese.

CRAFTY FALCON’s tactics see it go looking for internet-exposed environments used for testing or development, where it finds artefacts such as shared credentials, tokens, or network paths that help it move into production systems.

If it gets in, the crew re-writes victims’ applications so they do things like make unauthorized API calls. Victims’ defenses assume that only legitimate apps would call those APIs, so activity conducted by the re-written apps aren’t flagged as an attack.

Even if victims suspect an attack, they have a lot to cope with because CERT-IN says CRAFTY FALCON creates many “functionally identical variants refined in rapid succession; a pattern consistent with AI-generated code and iterative AI-assisted development.”

The crew also quickly exploits newly discovered vulnerabilities to keep its attacks fresh.

Once it gets a toehold, the criminal crew makes tiny payments of about $0.01 to test if its attacks work. If those transfers work, CRAFTY FALCON makes larger payments. When the target is a cryptocurrency exchange or virtual digital asset service provider, the crew steals wallet keys and empties cryptocurrency wallets.

Vendors of observability tools will be warming up their Indian sales teams today, because CERT-IN’s advice to Indian businesses includes improving their ability to log events, monitor for anomalies in real time, and send alerts. It also urged keeping test environments off the internet, and restricting access to cloud resources because CRAFTY FALCON is fond of using them to run its attacks.

CERT-IN also warns that the attacks are “active and escalating” and therefore worthy of 24x7 monitoring.

Interestingly, the CERT’s advisory mentions attacks on “entities” across banking, financial services, and insurance, but only names the kind of businesses mentioned above as having reported attacks.

The Register points this out because India is home to literally thousands of banks, many of them small operations that sometimes lack basic infosec protections. CRAFTY FALCON therefore has a target-rich environment in India should it choose to expand its activities. ®

Riley Green is just trying to find time to see his dog.

Riley Green is at the top of his game, with seven number one country songs, a spot as a coach on the long-running singing competition The Voice, currently in Season 30 on the 2026 TV schedule, and a well-received guest run on the Yellowstone spin-off Marshals. And while he might have recently made People Magazine’s Sexiest Man Alive issue, understandably so with his down-home appeal, he’s not looking to use that title to get himself out of bachelorhood anytime soon.

Whether he’s on tour as he takes his country crooning around the world or he’s in Los Angeles coaching a team of singers hoping to someday achieve the status of The Voice’s superstar coaches, Riley Green is booked and busy, and more concerned about finding time to spend with family than a girlfriend. While chatting with People about being selected for the sexiest list, he said he’s open to love but admitted to not even seeing his own dog in a month, so now is just probably not the best time. He explained:

I haven’t seen Carl, my dog, in a month. I struggle to think there’s a girl that, you know… I can barely make this lifestyle work. There’s so much growth in my career the last few years that that’s eventually going to stop. I mean, there is a level that I’ll get to and I’ll be completely content with it because I’ve already accomplished more than I ever thought I would... But I think it’s got to get to that place before I can really focus on me and what I want to do in my life.

It seems like the 38 year-old who likes to smack talk his fellow coaches on The Voice (especially O.G. American Idol winner Kelly Clarkson), has given the idea of love some definite thought. He’s very clear about the demands of his career and is riding that wave because he knows that his schedule likely won’t always be this hectic and the current high might not always be at the same level it is now.

For now, the “Worst Way” singer who even has his own customized Big Red Chair on The Voice is focusing on family. When you’re on the road 200-plus days a year, and also spending time in Los Angeles with coaching duties, hoping a member from Team Riley will take it all in Season 30, working in a FaceTime with your adorable nephew is about all the time you can spare. Green explained:

My lifestyle has just got to change a little bit. And I think that what gets me by now, without having those things that I’m kind of hoping for is, I get to FaceTime my nephew Joe, you know, and see what he’s doing and watch him play sports and catching up with my family. We’re all still really close, so they make it bearable in a pretty hectic lifestyle right now.

Constantly being on the road while touring can make any singer feel adrift, no matter how successful they are, so it’s great that Riley Green has close family that he can feel anchored by. Sure, the multi-talented performer who has added acting to his list of skills steals hearts and wins over the love of his growing fan base. But it’s probably a good thing that he wants to wait until he has the time to devote to focusing on just one woman before jumping into a relationship that he can’t fully be a part of. Autonomous combat drone maker Shield AI buys radar startup Echodyne for $5.3B

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Defense technology startup Shield AI Inc. said today it has agreed to buy the Bill Gates-backed radar startup Echodyne Inc. in a massive deal valued at $5.3 billion. The acquisition is intended to enhance Shield’s autonomous drone warfighting capabilities, it said.

Echodyne is a 13-year-old startup that’s best known for its Metamaterials Electronically Scanned Array or MESA technology, which is basically just a fancy name for its next-generation solid-state radar systems that can be integrated with a wide range of weapons and monitoring systems. The technology is designed to detect much smaller targets than traditional radar, including the kinds of compact autonomous drones that have terrorized soldiers on the battlefields of Ukraine and in other modern conflicts.

The deal comes just a couple of months after Echodyne landed a $250 million contract with the U.S. Defense Department’s counter-drone task force JIATF-401, which will see it provide drone tracking technology to the U.S. military.

Echodyne’s advanced radar systems look like a natural fit for Shield AI, which is the developer of the X-Bat and V-Bat vertical takeoff and landing systems, known as VTOL drones. Shield’s V-Bat hit the headlines in the last couple of years after assisting the U.S. Coast Guard in tracking down drug traffickers looking to bring illicit cargoes into the U.S. via speedboats, and notably participated in a number of large maritime drug busts. Shield AI is also the developer of Hivemind, an autonomous software system for piloting unmanned drones, as well as pilotless boats and spacecraft.

Shield AI Chief Executive Gary Steele said the deal will enable his company to accelerate its goal of branching into three different areas of unmanned systems. “We are building a new-generation defense technology company consisting of three fast-growing, high-margin, mutually supporting business units: autonomy software, autonomous systems and mission systems,” he said.

Echodyne co-founder and CEO Eben Frankenberg said Shield AI is a natural partner for his company because it shares the view that autonomous systems must be able to sense their environment to achieve their desired mission outcomes. “By joining Shield, we’re creating a company with the size and breadth to deliver even more value to our broad range of defense, government and commercial customers around the globe,” he insisted.

According to Steele, Echodyne will continue to operate as a standalone entity, as a separate business unit within Shield AI’s mission systems business. Frankenberg will remain with the company, where he will report to Steele as part of its senior executive team. Echodyne is not going to layoff any staff, and nobody will be asked to relocate either, he said. In the longer term, the companies expect tighter integration of Echodyne’s radar systems with Shield AI’s drones.

The deal will see $4.8 billion paid out upon closing, with the other $500 million dependent on Echodyne meeting certain performance targets, which have not been disclosed. Shield AI said it was able to fund the deal with a combination of its own cash and a $2.5 billion cash injection that came via a Series H funding round led by Blackstone and JPMorgan Chase, which was also announced today.

Shield AI has a history of making decisive acquisitions. Its V-Bat and X-Bat drones are heavily based on technology developed by a company called Martin UAV Systems Inc. that it acquired in 2021. In June, it purchased Aechelon Technologies Inc., a developer of high-fidelity simulation and physics-based sensor modeling software, for an undisclosed price.

Steele added that the company may not be done with acquisitions, and will continue to look at making more deals in order to accelerate its growth strategy.

Google, TikTok, and Meta are suing California to stop a law that bans “psychologically exploitative features” for kids under 16

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Google, TikTok, and Meta have each filed suit against California in an attempt to block enforcement of a law that protects kids under 16 from “psychologically exploitative features” on social media platforms.

Governor Gavin Newsom signed the law, Assembly Bill 1709, into law this past September. “We want our children–and every California ​kid–to grow up in a world where technology supports their wellbeing, rather than exploits their vulnerabilities,” Newsom said at the time.

AB 1709 prevents socmed platforms from being able to employ features like recommendation algorithms, infinite scroll, and video autoplay on accounts of users under the age of 16. Because of how Google, TikTok, and Meta have designed their platforms, it effectively bans kids under 16 from having accounts at all on YouTube, TikTok, Instagram, and Facebook.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

According to Reuters, the three big tech companies all said (in separate lawsuits) that California is wrongly forcing them to use a state-mandated method of delivering content to children, instead of leaving things up to parents’ discretion.

Their lawsuits claim violations of Section 230 (aka the “safe harbor” law that lets platforms off the liability hook for anything their users post) and First Amendment free speech protections.

“Parents ‌are best ⁠positioned to make the right decisions for their families,” Google spokesperson Jose Castaneda told Reuters. “Overly broad laws like this actually break the systems we’ve built to help kids find high-quality, age-appropriate videos.”

In its suit, TikTok said protected speech “cannot be suppressed solely to protect the young from ideas or images that a legislative body thinks is unsuitable.”

Meta’s suit, meanwhile, said California doesn’t have the right to override its editorial standards.

“We are challenging this law because it violates the First Amendment by letting the government dictate how ideas are organized, displayed or shared with Californians,” a Meta spokesperson told Courthouse News. “Under the law, we would be forced to either remove features that keep teens’ experiences age-appropriate, or cut thousands of California teens off from family, friends and their communities.”

TikTok’s suit said ​protected speech “cannot be suppressed solely to ​protect the young from ideas ⁠or images that a legislative body thinks unsuitable.”

“AB 1709 is a blatantly unconstitutional restriction on free expression that erodes the long-standing and protected practice of curating content based on user preferences, which book stores, TV networks and streaming services all use,” a TikTok spokesperson added. “Beyond disregarding real progress in making teens safer online, this law limits free speech and takes away parents’ rights to determine what’s appropriate for their kids.”

The defendant in all three suits is California Attorney General Rob Bonta; all three suits ask the court for an injunction that would stop him from enforcing AB 1709 against them.

Bonta’s office said it’s reviewing the suits, and that he is “committed to defending California’s commonsense laws to protect kids online.”

Jason Statham's 98-Minute James Bond-Esque Action Sequel Officially Hits Free Streaming

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jason Statham is a modern action movie star with a busy filmography spanning decades. He made his feature film debut in 1998 with Lock, Stock, and Two Smoking Barrels, where he played a small-time criminal called Bacon. Since then, he has starred in dozens of projects, from The Transporter and Crank to Fast & Furious Presents: Hobbs & Shaw.

Action movies are one of Statham's specialties. While some detractors may say his more recent movies are formulaic, fans of his work would agree that's part of the charm. To a certain extent, audiences know what they are getting with a Statham movie. Fans go in expecting to see him taking down scores of henchmen, looking cool while doing it, and that's precisely what they get.

Some of his newer movies arguably feel like they have 90s DNA. They are full of set pieces where Statham faces impossible odds against opponents with stormtrooper aim, and they are all the better for it. One movie that fits that description perfectly is 2016's Mechanic: Resurrection. Statham stars as Arthur Bishop. He's a mechanic, though, not in the traditional sense. Similar to The Beekeeper, his profession has a dual meaning. Bishop is actually a highly trained assassin, and he is called a Mechanic because he specializes in making his kills look like accidents.

Mechanic: Resurrection has many similarities to James Bond and spy thrillers. It's also currently available to stream for free on Tubi, with no subscription required.

Arthur Bishop may not work for MI6 with a license to kill, but he's been put in impossible situations only a special agent could escape. Bishop faked his own death at the end of The Mechanic, escaping to a quieter life. However, villains connected to his past saw through the facade. They then blackmail him into killing several high-profile targets, using his skills to make them look like accidents.

Bishop is forced to comply, as Crain (Sam Hazeldine) has taken Gina (Jessica Alba) hostage. Each kill in Mechanic: Resurrection is like a mini mission. Bishop must plan every hit with absolute precision and vanish without a trace. Like Bond, Bishop is resourceful. He sets up traps and takes down his enemies quietly, at least, until his cover is blown.

Bishop doesn't have Q Branch at his disposal, but he still relies on high-tech tools and gadgets to get the job done. Every kill takes place in a different environment, from a dingy prison to a secure high-rise building, and it's a treat to watch Bishop infiltrate each location while securing an escape route.

Mechanic: Resurrection starts where the original left off. The movie assumes you've watched the 2011 film, but it tells a solid standalone story that shouldn't leave audiences confused. As there's little backstory, Mechanic: Resurrection gets into the action almost immediately. It's a fast-paced movie, with many action set pieces crammed into its 98-minute runtime.

The 2016 movie has a satisfying conclusion, though it's a shame we never got a sequel. Mechanic: Resurrection performed well at the box office, grossing $125.7 million on a $40 million budget. It also outperformed the original. However, box office success doesn't guarantee a successful sequel. For example, Expend4bles was released in 2023 and took $51 million at the box office, a far cry from The Expendables 3's $209 million.

Without delving too deep into spoilers, Mechanic: Resurrection's ending offers a path to continue the story. However, it seems unlikely we will see Arthur Bishop return anytime soon. Jason Statham is working on multiple projects, including The Beekeeper 2, but there is no word of another Mechanic movie in the works. Still, Bishop's story has a good ending, even if we never get Mechanic 3, and you can enjoy Mechanic: Resurrection without worrying that it may lack a decent conclusion.

Prediction: This Is How Much XRP Will Be Worth by the End of 2030

📅 Nov 06, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It's time for investors to ratchet down their future growth expectations for XRP.

Future price targets for cryptocurrency XRP (XRP -6.61%) tend to be wildly bullish. Even though XRP is currently trading for just over $1, it's not uncommon to see price targets as high as $100. And, until recently, crypto bulls were convinced that XRP was heading to $1,000.

Unfortunately, XRP has consistently overpromised and under-delivered. In more than a decade of trading, XRP has never traded higher than $3.84.

XRP still has a chance to move higher over the next few years, but investors need to ratchet down their expectations. I predict XRP will only reach $2 by the end of 2030.

On the surface, a price target of $2 might sound underwhelming. After all, XRP traded as high as $3.65 last summer before eventually hitting a brick wall. Earlier in 2025, XRP hit $3.40 before giving back those gains.

But just think of the phenomenal growth rate XRP will need to regain the $4 price level over the next few years. To move from its current price of $1 to a future price of $4, XRP will need to grow at a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of more than 40%.

To put that number into context, Bitcoin has delivered a 37% CAGR over the past decade, while Ethereum has delivered a 23% CAGR. So investors are asking XRP to grow faster than either Bitcoin or Ethereum, the two market leaders.

With that in mind, a more realistic growth rate for XRP may be 20%. That's still faster than the growth rate of many established tech companies. If XRP grows at a 20% CAGR over the next four years, it will double in value to $2 by the end of 2030.

Of course, it will take a lot for XRP to achieve a 20% growth rate. First and foremost, new use cases need to be found for the XRP token, which is primarily used now as a bridge currency for cross-border transactions. One of the most interesting new use cases could be real-world asset (RWA) tokenization on the XRP blockchain ledger.

Secondly, the regulatory environment for crypto needs to remain supportive, and new crypto legislation needs to be passed to make it easier for financial institutions and banks to embrace blockchain-based payment technology. It looked like this crypto legislation would pass this year, but the Clarity Act fizzled in the Senate in September.

If both catalysts deliver as planned, XRP could finally see a breakout. But keep your expectations in check. XRP is not going to hit $10, $100, or $1,000. Instead, XRP will be fortunate to double in value and reach $2 by the end of 2030.

Dominic Basulto has positions in Bitcoin, Ethereum, and XRP. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Bitcoin, Ethereum, and XRP. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Nasdaq sell-off batters Asian chip stocks as SK Hynix, Samsung, Softbank, TSMC tumble

📅 2026-11-06  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Asian technology stocks tumbled Friday, tracking a sharp sell-off on Wall Street overnight that sent the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite into correction territory as renewed concerns over artificial intelligence spending rattled investor sentiment.

South Korean chipmaker SK Hynix plunged over 8%, while Samsung Electronics shed 7%. LG Innotek and LG Display fell 10% and 7.7%, respectively, as selling pressure spread across the country's technology sector.

The rout came as investors worried that Democrats' sweeping victory in the U.S. midterm elections could lead to tighter restrictions on data-center development, potentially slowing demand for AI chips and related infrastructure.

In Japan, semiconductor-related stocks also fell, with chip-testing equipment maker Advantest plunging 12.2%, while Tokyo Electron slid 7.3%. Technology investment giant SoftBank Group tumbled 11.6%.

Shares of memory chipmaker Kioxia Holdings fell nearly 10%, while Renesas Electronics declined 7.5%.

In Taiwan, semiconductor stocks also came under heavy selling pressure, with contract chipmaker TSMC sliding 9.6%. Electronics manufacturers Hon Hai Precision Industry, also known as Foxconn, and Foxconn Technology fell 9.4% and 9.1%, respectively, as the regional technology rout deepened.

The declines followed a brutal session on Wall Street, where the Nasdaq Composite sank 5.5%, amid mounting concerns over the sustainability of the AI investment boom and returns on massive data-center spending. It closed more than 10% below its recent peak. The Philadelphia Semiconductor Index plunged 12.2%.

The sell-off in Asian equities has been exacerbated by investors unwinding positions, stop-loss selling and heightened volatility, Timothy Moe, chief Asia Pacific equity strategist at Goldman Sachs, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Asia" on Friday.He added that potential political opposition to U.S. data center construction may affect demand and profits for Asian AI hardware suppliers.

"If there's concern about the speed and magnitude of buildout in the U.S., then obviously that's going to put all that into question," he said. "The market's tendency is always to shoot first and ask questions later. I think that's exactly what's happening now."

Square Peg Backs Enterprise AI Co-Worker Developer Future Secure AI

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Square Peg announced on November 6, 2026, that it is backing Future Secure AI, whose enterprise AI Co-Workers are designed to complete complex business processes alongside employees and inside customers’ existing systems.

Future Secure AI designs, builds and runs these multi-agent systems. Each Co-Worker has a name, a face and a place on the organization chart, while underlying agents handle intake, judgment calls, system updates and hand-offs. Deterministic logic is used where a language model is the wrong tool, with human involvement where appropriate.

The company calls its target area “Red Zone” AI: workflows that cross multiple systems and teams and require accuracy, security and oversight. Examples include month-end close, tax calculations, reconciliations and customer service, which can sit inside decades-old systems of record and behind strict data rules. That makes completion of the whole operating process, rather than faster responses to individual prompts, the central product requirement.

Delivery is built around executive relationships and embedded implementation. Future Secure AI sells to senior executives, CEOs and boards, opening with their largest business problems. Senior former consultants and engineers then work with customer teams to scope, build and operate the Co-Workers. This forward-deployed model ties the software to a team responsible for fitting it into a customer’s actual processes.

Future Secure AI says its AI Co-Workers execute complex, high-volume workflows instead of only individual tasks, and describes them as LLM-agnostic, resilient and fully auditable. Each is purpose-built for a business role and tailored to an organization’s people, processes and systems. Named examples include Amy AI, a finance month-end analyst for a global investment bank; Ari AI, a fringe benefits tax analyst for a global investment bank; Leo AI, a rostering manager for a medical diagnostic company; and Billie AI, a billing assistant for a global law firm.

Future Secure AI says its technology is not tied to a single large language model, cloud provider or enterprise platform, and is designed to integrate with a customer’s existing environment. Deployments can use single-tenant private-cloud infrastructure and containerized applications inside an enterprise network. Under that configuration, sensitive data remains within the environment and is not used to train public AI models.

The company describes an operating system that coordinates long-running workflows across systems, approvals, decisions and business processes. Its controls include configurable guardrails, monitoring and human oversight, together with operational visibility, explainability and audit trails. Lifecycle management covers deployment, monitoring, support and continuing optimization. Employees can collaborate with Co-Workers through email, voice, Slack and Teams, while connected data access covers structured and unstructured information.

Future Secure AI’s leadership page identifies Mehrdad Baghai and Michael Hunter as co-founders and co-CEOs. Baghai’s biography describes him as a former McKinsey partner and an adviser to CEOs and boards, while Hunter’s background covers entrepreneurship and investment banking at Macquarie Capital.

Square Peg’s account adds that Baghai co-led McKinsey’s worldwide Growth Practice and co-wrote The Alchemy of Growth, later running growth and commercialisation at CSIRO, Australia’s national science agency. Hunter spent more than a decade as a Macquarie investment banker working closely with senior leadership. Those careers help explain why the investor views executive relationships and credibility as part of the company’s delivery model.

Square Peg’s thesis is that enterprise AI adoption will combine products and services, that suppliers must define business problems and own delivery, and that technology alone will not determine who wins complex, regulated workflows where executive trust and delivery proof points are central. That view connects its backing to the founders’ backgrounds and Future Secure AI’s forward-deployed teams, beyond the capabilities of the underlying models.

For Future Secure AI, Square Peg’s backing is an endorsement of an enterprise-AI model built around execution, integration and governance. The central test is whether its role-specific Co-Workers and senior delivery teams can turn those controls into dependable operation across the Red Zone workflows it targets.

If the White House Network Can Be Hijacked by Proxies, Your Smart Home Is Already at Risk

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Tech > Services & Software > Cybersecurity

A new report finds residential proxies exiting the White House network 236 times in the past month.

As if the Trump administration needed any more bad news this week, cybersecurity researchers discovered several residential proxy services originating from the White House network, exposing a potential security flaw that could allow cybercriminals to route traffic through official government IP addresses.

The security firm Layer3 Intel detected residential proxy activity on AS2650, a network registered to the Executive Office of the President. Another security firm identified software from the proxy server Infatica, a company owned by a Russian entrepreneur who ran a server-rental company used by hackers in attacks on election systems in Arizona and Illinois in 2016.

While there’s no evidence that proxy access was used to carry out attacks on the White House, experts say it could open the door for hackers to exploit vulnerabilities or grant outsiders an unmonitored back door into high-security systems.

“It basically gives them a foothold on a network where previously nobody was getting into the White House network,” Layer3 founder Sergiy Usatyuk told CNET. “From a security policy perspective, it gives you all types of potential pathways to exploit.” (Usatyuk pleaded guilty to federal charges in 2019 for his role in operating websites that launched denial-of-service, or DDoS, attacks.)

Residential proxy services route internet traffic through consumer devices (such as smartphones, laptops or home routers) to make it appear that the activity is being performed by a real person. While proxies can be used for relatively benign purposes — web scraping is by far the most common use case — they’re also popular for activities like malware attacks, identity theft and creating fake social media accounts.

For example, earlier this year, Synthient, another company that tracks proxies and VPNs, documented how one botnet infected more than 2 million devices using a residential proxy server. If a similar vulnerability existed in the White House proxy, hackers could probe the infected device’s internal systems and install malicious payloads.

In its report, Layer3 found traffic from six distinct IP addresses exiting the White House network from at least 28 known residential proxy services between Oct. 5 and Nov. 3, with most activity occurring in the evenings between 5 p.m. and midnight EST. This suggests the culprit may be an employee who connected a personal device to a Wi-Fi network with a compromised app or a proxy SDK (software development kit) running in the background. Third-party SDKs can convert a device into a “proxy node.”

Commercial proxy networks sell access to these nodes, so anyone with a few dollars could rent a trusted IP address — in this case, making it appear as though traffic originates from the Executive Office of the President.

Researchers at Synthient provided more details about the case, posting on X that several White House staffers are running “a Windows 11 machine with InfaticaSDK installed.” They pointed directly to Rave, a popular app that lets users watch streaming services with friends, as the likely delivery mechanism for the residential proxy traffic detected on the White House network.

Riley Kilmer, co-founder of the cybersecurity firm Spur Intelligence, wrote in a LinkedIn post that different types of proxy companies had been discovered in the White House, including one called 9Proxy, which is often used by criminals for banking fraud.

The White House didn’t respond to CNET’s request for comment.

Because smart TVs, connected appliances and home routers usually have minimal user oversight, a single unvetted app or out-of-date firmware update can turn a household of IoT devices into an exit node for commercial proxy networks.

Proxies can generally “glom” onto consumer devices by embedding networking code directly into software, either consensually (users willingly install it) or nonconsensually (by exploiting unsecured device access points).

It starts when app developers look for revenue by integrating those proxy SDKs into their free apps. Then, when you install the app, the fine print of the terms and conditions of service often includes a clause that allows the app to add your IP address to a third-party proxy pool.

A June report from Spur Intelligence found that roughly one in three apps available on Samsung and LG smart TVs were selling users’ IP addresses. To avoid ads, users often accept the terms when downloading the app — a type of unnoticed consent. Once installed, the SDK runs continuously in the background, registering the device’s IP with the proxy provider’s gateway, unless you delete the app or opt out. (The report notes that Amazon and Roku have explicitly banned this practice.)

Using a free VPN could expose you to the same risk, allowing your IP address to be used by someone else for nefarious activities. The FBI’s guidance on protecting yourself against proxies specifically calls out free VPNs as one of the primary ways bad actors gain access to an IP address.

In some cases, you don’t need to download anything to get your IP thrown into a shady proxy pool — you just have to buy the wrong device. A recent investigation by Synthient discovered that more than 1.3 million streaming devices from the China-based device manufacturer MECOOL were infected with proxy SDKs. A proxy service called SocksEscort was charged by the Justice Department in March with compromising thousands of routers to submit fraudulent unemployment insurance claims.

Checking whether your IP address is being used by a proxy service without your knowledge is relatively simple. Layer3 provides a free proxy-checking tool that searches its database of over 70 million proxies to find if yours is listed. GreyNoise, another cybersecurity company, also offers a free IP-checking service.

These tools can tell you if proxy traffic is passing through your network, but they can’t identify which specific device has the SDK running. You’ll have to do some additional detective work to figure out the culprit. Some common sources include free VPNs, gaming apps, “one price, no subscriptions” streaming boxes and smart TVs.

Cybersecurity experts also recommend keeping the firmware on all your internet-connected devices up to date and downloading only from trusted app stores.

PEARL integrates with NEAR Intents to expand cross-chain access for AI miners — Can demand follow?

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Pearl, the native token of the Pearl Layer-1 network, has launched on NEAR Intents. The move allows for swapping of tokens across more than 35 different networks. Additionally, the integration provides the Near Protocol’s [NEAR] Click API.

As a result, this makes it possible for wallets and applications to integrate Pearl [PEARL] token swaps.

In simple words, through NEAR’s infrastructure, miners will be able to swap PEARL for assets within all of those 35+ networks. Therefore, there may be easier access to stablecoins or other currencies for miners as they pay for electricity and hardware.

More importantly, Pearl’s Proof of Useful Work model links token issuance to AI computations that also secure the network. As such, miners receive both block rewards and inference fees based upon the same workload they are using to mine blocks.

This link could create a direct relationship between AI usage and token utility. However, for PEARL to have sustained value, it will require consistent AI-related workloads creating sufficient activity and generating fees that can support miners.

As part of the integration, NEAR intents provides PEARL with access to an existing cross-chain market. No additional cross-chain bridges for each individual network would be required for this.

Currently, NEAR intents’ ecosystem provides a cross-chain marketplace that includes approximately 38 networks, along with more than 200 assets. Major stablecoin markets are included in the asset list as well.

This is important for miners who need to have liquid means to exit transactions quickly in order to meet costs associated with electricity and hardware. NEAR intents reported more than $37 billion in cumulative volume and more than $144 million in daily volume traded.

A large trading platform like this offers stability for those looking to utilize cross-chain swaps.

Moreover, confidential transactions enable users to transfer funds anonymously. Although the fact that the entire network is utilizing this function does not necessarily mean that there will be a greater demand for PEARL.

Instead, it will rely upon how frequently miners and traders utilize these services to conduct swaps, develop deeper liquidity levels, and ensure reliable execution.

PEARL’s next challenge is converting wider access into sustained market demand. PEARL’s market activity will show whether easier cross-chain access translates into genuine demand. At press time, the token was trading around $1.69, up 17% in the last 24 hours.

The daily volume increased alongside price rally, hovering near $12 million and was up 36%.

However, activity remains concentrated on a few centralized exchanges, suggesting NEAR access has yet to reshape its liquidity. This matters because GPU miners need reliable markets to convert rewards into operating funds.

Ultimately, stronger demand must therefore come from real AI workloads and paid inference, not token issuance alone.

PEARL (PEARL) has joined NEAR Intents, gaining confidential swap access across more than 35 blockchain networks.

PEARL’s long-term demand will depend on real AI workloads, paid inference, and sustained trading activity.

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
Oscar Health boasts ‘strongest year’ in its history after outperforming in Q3" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

Oscar Health is seeing the “strongest year” in its history in 2026, CEO Mark Bertolini told investors during the company’s third quarter earnings call Thursday morning.

“We delivered record profitability for the first nine months of the year, reporting earnings from operations of $1.3 billion and net income of $1.2 billion,” he said. “We also expect to achieve several of our 2024 Investor Day commitments one year ahead of plan.”

Oscar Health brought in $5.1 billion in Q3 revenue, up “roughly 70%” year-over-year, Bertolini said. It beat analysts’ estimates of $4.72 billion.

Its medical loss ratio was 84%, down from 88.5% year-over-year, according to financial results published Thursday morning.

The company ended Q3 with 2.7 million members, up 27% year-over-year, Bertolini said, adding it is entering open enrollment with a “24% larger footprint” with plans to expand into South Carolina. “Our expansion increases our total addressable market to approximately 10 million consumers across 21 states,” he said.

Moreover, Oscar Health is launching a “new wave” of consumer lifestyle products and “addressing affordability through bundled benefits,” Bertolini said.

He also pointed to new artificial intelligence capabilities across the Oscar Health platform, including Lucie. Unveiled in April, the platform is a one-stop shop for individuals seeking health plans and supplemental benefit options.

“No other platform brings these capabilities together in one place, he said. “Lucie is on track to sell approximately 800,000 new policies in 2027, driven by growing broker and consumer demand.”

Based on the company’s performance, CFO Scott Blackley said Oscar Health is raising its 2026 full year outlook. Earnings are expected to be in a range of $850 million to $950 million, Blackley said.

Total revenue is expected to be in the range of $19 billion to $19.3 billion, which Blackley notes is “an increase of $300 million” from its prior outlook.

Blackley continued that the company expects to end the year with 2.5 million members, a figure that reflects “additional disenrollments from the second CMS review.” A full-year MLR of 80.5% to 81.5% is also expected.

The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel Merch Is Already Here

Sam Woods is TheGamer’s Senior Reporter. He’s been in games journalism since 2020 and has undertaken a variety of roles in that time. These have included roles such as SEO Editor, Managing Editor at DualShockers, and Global Head of Gaming, and he's also been seen on the BBC. He was promoted to the Senior Reporter position in 2025 following a series of high-quality reports and exclusives for TheGamer.

Since 2020, Sam has written over 3,000 articles and specializes in telling the stories of the people behind the games. He’s interviewed the biggest game directors, some of the industry’s top talents, told the stories of how indie games came to be, and spoken to players about what really makes them tick. He’s also interviewed some of the world’s biggest musical artists to find out how the medium has inspired their work, and TV and Film actors about their roles in video game adaptations. He even hosted a PAX panel in 2021 and made a documentary in 2024 surrounding the creation of a Disney game.

Alongside this, Sam focuses on breaking news. He got the scoop on Perfect Dark’s cancellation, provided context on some AI drama, and revealed that Lady Dimitrescu’s actress would love to step back into the character’s giant shoes. These articles have been picked up by the biggest gaming sites, including IGN, GameSpot, and PC Gamer. He also loves to dive into the numbers behind the games.

Sam’s journey in games journalism has taken him all over the world. He’s covered Gamescom in Germany, Summer Game Fest in Los Angeles, and Tokyo Game Show in Japan, each on three occasions.

He’s been learning Japanese since 2024 with the intention of identifying and translating Japanese-language news and bringing it to English-language audiences first, something he’s done countless times.

Sam’s first review was 2020’s Battletoads reboot, and he’s since reviewed games in The Legend of Zelda, Super Mario, and Pokemon franchises.

Gaming is the most profitable form of media in the world, and for good reason. While game sales clearly make big bucks, it's the potential for merchandise sales that really gets investors' tails wagging. Look at something like Pokemon, the highest-grossing media franchise ever: it's made $40 billion from its home video games, $10 billion from mobile games, and a staggering $119.3 billion from "retail sales".

It makes sense that every company wants to get on the merchandise train, then, and Bethesda is no different. We've seen it release tons of merchandise for Fallout and The Elder Scrolls series, and it's starting early with the next Elder Scrolls game. Just two days after it was named, The Elder Scrolls 6 has received an expensive T-shirt and hoodie.

On Tuesday, after a couple of months of speculation and a rather big hint from Xbox CEO Asha Sharma, The Elder Scrolls 6 officially got its subtitle: Sentinel. Eight years after its original reveal, we got the most details we've ever received: the name and the game's setting, which happens to be Hammerfell.

These limited details haven't stopped Bethesda and Microsoft from trying to capitalize on the hype and make a quick buck, though, as a pair of Sentinel-themed clothing items have just hit the Bethesda store.

The first is a plain black T-shirt, emblazoned with The Elder Scrolls 6: Sentinel logo displayed above. That's available for the low, low price of $35. The T-shirt is accompanied on the website by a black hoodie, once again bearing the Sentinel logo. The hoodie costs $65.

Behind Grand Theft Auto 6, The Elder Scrolls 6 is probably the most anticipated game yet to release—and the eight years since the first teaser was revealed probably haven't helped with that. It's undeniable that the game will be huge and do gangbusters, The Elder Scrolls fans have been starved (outside of 142 re-releases of Skyrim), but it sure does feel a little early to be releasing expensive merchandise. I guess a development cycle this long isn't cheap, though.

What a courier success rate actually tells a cash-on-delivery seller

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

75%, 45%, and the two-parcel trap: turning one number into a shipping decision.

In Bangladesh, most online orders are paid in cash on delivery. When a parcel comes back, the seller pays the delivery charge, the return charge and the time. So before confirming an order, many sellers look up the customer's phone number in a courier fraud checker and see a success rate: the share of that number's past parcels that were delivered.

The number is useful. The problem is what people do with it.

I build WooEasyLife, and our free checker colours results in four bands. Here is what I suggest a seller does in each:

A ratio means little when it is built on very few parcels. One return out of two parcels is 50%. Ten returns out of twenty is also 50%. They are not the same customer. Always look at the total number of parcels next to the percentage.

A customer can have a clean record with one courier and many returns with another. Looking at a single courier's panel hides that pattern.

Parcels come back for many reasons: a wrong address, a switched-off phone, a late delivery. A low success rate is a reason to take one more step before you ship. It is not proof of anything about the person.

My Bangla guide on this topic used to say "70%+ is good", while the checker itself used 75% and 45%. Two numbers on one site help nobody. The guide now reads its thresholds from the checker's code, and a test fails if they ever drift apart.

The full guide (in Bangla) and the free checker are on app.wpsalehub.com.

I built a cross-API rate limit reference because I was tired of guessing

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I kept hitting 429s on APIs I use daily. Every time, I'd search "Stripe rate limit" or "OpenAI rate limit" and land on a docs page that told me about one API, in one tier, with no error shape and no retry logic.

So I started writing down what I actually needed:

The first page covers OpenAI's 2026 limits — Build / Launch / Grow tiers, the difference between slow_down (429) and server_is_overloaded (503), and a Python retry wrapper that respects Retry-After instead of blindly backing off. Every number is dated.

It's here if it's useful: https://api-ceilings.github.io/theceiling/

No signup. No tracking. Just the numbers and the sources. I'll be adding more APIs one at a time — if there's a specific API whose limits you keep hitting, tell me and I'll prioritize it.

After the SOC Assessment: Common Findings and How to Fix Them

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

_ Got your SOC assessment report back? Here are the findings that show up most often and a practical plan to remediate them._

The report lands in your inbox. Forty pages, a heat map, and a list of findings that wasn't there last week. Now what?

This is the stage where an assessment either pays off or collects dust. Let's walk through the issues that appear most often, how to rank them, and how to turn them into progress. If you're working with a team offering SOC consulting, this is also where good advisors earn their keep.

Every environment is different, but certain patterns repeat.

1. Blind spots in log coverage. Critical systems, cloud workloads, or identity platforms aren't feeding the monitoring tool. Teams often assume they are, because someone set it up years ago.

2. Noisy, untuned alerts. Analysts drown in low-value alerts, so real ones get missed or delayed. Alert fatigue is one of the biggest silent risks.

3. Playbooks that exist only in theory. Documents are outdated, never tested, or unknown to half the team.

4. Weak escalation paths. Contact lists are stale. Nobody's sure who can authorize isolating a server at midnight.

5. Thin after-hours coverage. Strong daytime performance, thin or no coverage overnight and on weekends.

6. Limited detection mapped to real attacker behavior. Rules focus on a few familiar threats while whole categories of technique go unwatched.

7. Inconsistent documentation in tickets. Investigations lack notes, making handoffs and post-incident reviews painful.

8. No meaningful metrics. The team counts alerts but can't say how fast it detects or responds.

9. Tool sprawl and poor integration. Analysts bounce between consoles, wasting time and losing context.

10. Skills and burnout concerns. High turnover, limited training, and heavy workloads.

If you recognize several of these, you're in very ordinary company.

Resist the temptation to start fixing everything. Sort first.

Use two simple lenses: risk (how bad is it if this goes unaddressed?) and effort (how hard is the fix?). That gives you four buckets:

Be honest about risk. Findings tied to detection of serious threats outrank cosmetic documentation issues.

These won't solve everything, but they build momentum and visibly reduce risk.

You don't have to do it all alone. Outside support can make sense for specific needs:

When evaluating remediation help, look for advisors who explain trade-offs and teach your team along the way, not those who create dependency. The goal is a stronger in-house capability or a well-managed partnership, not a permanent black box.

Remediation projects often fade after the first burst of energy. A few habits help.

Name an owner. Someone senior should be accountable for progress.

Track it visibly. A simple board or dashboard works.

Review monthly. Short check-ins catch stalls early.

Celebrate wins. Acknowledge when a gap closes. It reinforces the work.

Retest. Verify that fixes actually work. A closed ticket isn't proof.

Executives want to know three things: how risky is it, what will it cost, and how will we know it's working. Frame your plan around those.

Show the findings in terms of business impact, such as slower detection of account compromise or exposure of customer data. Present a phased roadmap with rough costs and expected risk reduction. Offer a small set of metrics that will demonstrate progress over time.

Treating the report as the finish line. The assessment is the start.

Fixing the easy stuff only. Quick wins matter, but don't ignore the hard, high-risk items.

Buying a tool to solve a process problem. Often the issue is workflow or tuning, not software.

Skipping validation. Test that changes behave as intended.

Blaming individuals. Findings usually reflect systemic gaps, not personal failures.

Picture a healthcare services firm whose assessment flagged missing identity logs, noisy alerts, and no formal after-hours coverage. They spent the first month updating contacts and tuning the five loudest rules. By month three they'd onboarded identity logs and run a tabletop exercise that exposed unclear authority for isolating systems. By month nine they had an on-call rotation and a simple set of response metrics.

Nothing dramatic, just steady, prioritized work. At their reassessment, three domains had moved up a maturity level, and the team could point to numbers instead of impressions.

A report is only as valuable as what happens next. Sort findings by risk and effort, grab the quick wins, plan the heavier lifts, and keep ownership clear. Do that consistently, and the next assessment becomes less of an interrogation and more of a progress check.

The Same TSA Scanner That Lets Laptops Stay In Your Bag Is Quietly Ruining Other Gear

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

While some passengers might not directly notice it, airport security checkpoints across the United States are changing. For most travelers, the most visible benefit is simple: laptops, tablets, and approved liquids can increasingly stay inside carry-on bags, removing one of the most familiar airport security rituals of the past two decades. However, this same technology that makes the checkpoint feel more convenient also creates new problems for some travelers.

The Transportation Security Administration (TSA) has been rolling out advanced computed tomography, commonly known as CT, scanners as part of a major security technology upgrade. In March 2022, the agency awarded two orders totaling up to $781.2 million for base and full-size CT X-ray systems for airport checkpoints, while later procurement added even more units to the program. These machines are designed to improve threat detection by creating three-dimensional images of carry-on bags.

TSA officers can rotate and inspect those images in ways that were not possible with older two-dimensional X-ray machines. For the average passenger, this means fewer items need to be removed from bags, while for travelers carrying analog film, overstuffed carry-ons, or other sensitive items, the story is less straightforward.

The new CT scanning equipment represents a major shift from the traditional X-ray equipment passengers have become used to at airport checkpoints. Older systems produced flatter images, making it harder for officers to distinguish between items layered together inside a bag. This is one reason laptops and other large electronics often need to be removed and screened separately.

The introduction of newer CT systems, which use more advanced imaging and algorithms to create 3D views of carry-on content, is increasingly changing this. According to TSA, officers can view and rotate the image on three axes to identify possible threat items more effectively. The agency has described the technology as both a security upgrade and a way to improve the passenger experience. Today, these scanners are already available at some of the largest hubs in the United States, including Hartsfield-Jackson Atlanta International Airport (ATL), Chicago O'Hare International Airport (ORD), and New York JFK Airport (JFK), among many others.

For travelers, the most obvious change is the reduced need to unpack their hand luggage at the conveyor belt. TSA has stated that under current screening procedures for CT lanes, laptops are allowed to remain in bags. In some airport press releases, the agency has also noted that passengers screened through CT units do not need to remove 3-1-1 liquids or laptops. Travelers no longer need to place laptops at the top of a backpack just to remove them quickly, and toiletry bags do not always need to be kept in an outside pocket.

The downside is that CT scanners do not treat every item the same way, and their more powerful imaging is useful for security screening, but it can be a problem for undeveloped photographic materials. This is where the new technology creates a completely different experience for analog photographers than for passengers carrying digital equipment. The new CT scanners can damage or destroy unprocessed film rolls, and the stronger imaging can cause damage in a single screening. CT scanners can fog unprocessed rolls, reduce image quality, and cause permanent damage, forcing analog photographers to change how they move through security.

However, this does not mean that every piece of camera equipment is at risk; digital cameras, lenses, batteries, and memory cards are not affected in the same way. The concern is specifically undeveloped film, including rolls carried separately, film already loaded inside a camera, disposable analog cameras, and instant materials. For most travelers, this may sound like a niche problem, but analog photography has become increasingly popular again among travelers who want a different look from digital smartphone photos. Many unaware passengers might also fail to realize there is a risk until after their trip, when the damage is already done.

Airport security technology has changed greatly over the last 25 years.

As a result of the new scanners, the most important rule for travelers carrying analog materials is simple: do not allow it to go through a CT scanner if it can be avoided. TSA’s own guidance recommends placing undeveloped film and cameras containing undeveloped film in carry-on bags, or taking undeveloped film to the checkpoint and asking for a hand inspection.

That means film should not be buried at the bottom of a suitcase. Instead, it should be packed so it's easy to remove before reaching the scanner. It is also advisable to separate unprocessed rolls from cameras where possible and place them in transparent, quart-sized zipper bags on top of the carry-on. That makes it easier for TSA officers to inspect without delaying the process and the entire line behind.

The hand inspection is not especially complicated, but it does take a bit of time, since TSA officers generally take the film to a separate area, visually inspect each roll, swab it for explosive residue, and then manually clear it. The process typically takes three to 10 minutes, with passengers advised to arrive around 15 to 20 minutes earlier if they know they will need the extra step. It is important to note that travelers should clearly tell the TSA officer that they are carrying undeveloped photographic materials and would like a hand inspection.

TSA PreCheck Touchless ID is available at 65 airports nationwide and supports more than 100 participating airlines

The new CT machines have also changed the carry-on versus checked-bag decision process. Many passengers assume that fragile or valuable items should stay in the cabin, but when flying with film, there is another reason to avoid checked baggage. Checked luggage is also screened using similar powerful equipment, and passengers generally do not have the same opportunity to request a hand inspection once the bag disappears behind the check-in counter.

For that reason, film travelers should keep undeveloped rolls, disposable cameras, and cameras containing film in their hand luggage. This does not mean placing them deep inside a trolley that may need to be gate-checked later. The safest option is to keep film in a personal item or small carry-on that will remain accessible through the checkpoint.

There is also a practical difference between domestic and international travel. TSA guidance applies in the United States, where passengers can request a hand inspection. Procedures abroad may vary, and foreign airport security staff may not always handle film requests in the same way. For travelers connecting internationally, that means planning ahead and expecting the same conversation more than once.

The 100ml rule is finally being scrapped after last year's false start.

Besides the potential risk to analog film, newer CT scanning equipment can also create problems for travelers carrying oversized or overstuffed bags, since the scanners have fixed tunnel openings. This means a bag that does not physically fit through the machine cannot be screened in that lane.

That matters because many passengers have become used to a certain amount of flexibility around carry-on sizes. Most major US airlines use a standard domestic carry-on limit of around 22 x 14 x 9 inches (56 x 36 x 23 cm). However, in practice, the strictness of the enforcement has often depended on the airline policy, gate agent, aircraft type, and how full the flight is. Soft-sided bags and expanded trolleys could sometimes slip through, especially when passengers were not asked to place them in a sizing frame.

However, the CT scanners at security lanes are now changing that dynamic, and if a carry-on is too large for the scanner, the issue arises at the security checkpoint rather than at the gate. In that sense, the new scanning technology has become an indirect baggage-size enforcement tool for airlines, and while passengers might not be trying to break a rule, an expanded carry-on suitcase that once passed through older equipment may now be pulled aside before screening can even continue.

The discovery of two live smoke grenades hidden inside a jar of peanut butter in a passenger's checked luggage highlights the importance of screening.

For passengers, the CT rollout at TSA checkpoints is making security increasingly convenient for the average passenger but less forgiving for travelers carrying certain items. Laptops and liquids staying in the bag is a real improvement, as it avoids the need to unpack half a bag in public, but it does not mean passengers can stop thinking about how their carry-on is packed.

A carry-on now needs to be packed not only to meet the airline’s baggage rules but also to fit within the physical limits of the checkpoint itself. For most travelers, the best strategy is to keep laptops and liquids where they fit naturally, while making sure the bag remains within airline carry-on dimensions and is not expanded beyond its normal size. For passengers traveling with analog cameras and photo rolls, it is important they remain accessible and separated where possible. This ensures it is ready to be handed to an officer for a manual inspection before the bag enters the scanner.

The best approach is therefore not to assume every checkpoint will work the same way. Travelers should still arrive prepared for traditional lanes, especially at airports where older and newer machines operate side by side. At the same time, those carrying analog photographic materials or borderline-sized bags should plan around the newer system.
