Napoli, Allegri sta perdendo la squadra: Hojlund lo smentisce, Politano spacca lo spogliatoio

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Massimiliano Allegri assolve i giocatori, Rasmus Hojlund non assolve Massimiliano Allegri. E Matteo Politano, nel frattempo, apre una distinzione che fino a ieri poteva anche esistere nello spogliatoio del Napoli, ma almeno non era stata portata all'esterno: italiani contro stranieri, una sorta di tutti contro tutti da cui diventerà complicato uscire. Il 4-1 di Porto, settima sconfitta nelle prime quattordici partite stagionali degli azzurri, ha fatto emergere un problema che rischia di essere persino più grave dei risultati. Allegri non ha ancora perso la squadra, ma rischia di perderla. E l'emergenza infortuni, per quanto pesantissima, non basta a spiegare quello che sta succedendo.

L'allenatore, dopo la partita, ha tentato di proteggere i suoi. Ha parlato di una prestazione positiva per settanta minuti, ha spiegato di non avere nulla da rimproverare ai giocatori. Hojlund, interrogato proprio sulle parole del tecnico, lo ha contraddetto senza girarci intorno: "Non sono d'accordo". Non è una dichiarazione scontata: il centravanti danese non ha cercato giustificazioni, ha definito negativa la prestazione e ha chiesto a tutti di guardarsi allo specchio, società compresa. Una lettura diametralmente opposta a quella di Max, espressa pubblicamente e a pochi minuti di distanza. Se non è una contestazione tecnica, quantomeno è tutto il contrario che messaggio che Allegri aveva appena provato a far passare.

Se Hojlund mette in discussione la lettura di Allegri, Politano rischia di andare oltre, e spaccare gli equilibri del gruppo. O magari ha solo raccontato una frattura che già esiste. Alla richiesta di spiegare l'ennesima sconfitta, l'esterno ha manifestato il proprio fastidio per essere sempre tra quelli chiamati a metterci la faccia: vorrebbe che lo facessero anche altri, "non sempre noi italiani". Poi ha precisato di non voler attaccare nessuno e ha ribadito la vicinanza all'allenatore. Ma quella distinzione resta. Soprattutto in uno spogliatoio nel quale gli stranieri non sono certo comprimari: De Bruyne, McTominay, Hojlund, Anguissa, tanto per citarne alcuni. A dire il vero, poi sono andati davanti ai microfoni il già citato Hojlund e proprio McTominay che, a sua volta, ha chiesto a ogni giocatore di interrogarsi sul proprio contributo. Il nervosismo, peraltro, non ha aspettato le interviste post gara. De Bruyne e Lucca hanno litigato in campo, con il centravanti che ha risposto platealmente alle rimostranze del belga. Un episodio che isolatamente avrebbe avuto scarso significato, ma che assume un peso diverso nel contesto attuale. I nodi stanno venendo al petto: il Napoli è fragile in campo, soprattutto rischia di diventarlo nei rapporti interni. La partita con la Lazio sarà importante anche per capire se Allegri abbia ancora la capacità di tenere tutti dalla stessa parte. Perché gli infortunati prima o poi torneranno. Ricomporre eventuali fratture, invece, potrebbe essere più complicato.

British football's top 25 most intimidating football stadiums ranked by AI

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There's always some stadiums in British football which are notably tougher to pick up three points from.

Of course, this can come down to quality on the pitch between the two teams, but also at the same time, having an intimidating atmosphere at home can make any visiting team fear the worst.

We've seen home teams use this fiery atmosphere on home soil to their advantage over the years, which has seen results improve in front of the home crowd, which gives them much-needed momentum heading into the later stages of a season.

We asked AI to rank British football's top 25 most intimidating football stadiums, and below, you'll find the first stadium they mentioned at number 25.

The Bluebirds’ support carries a fierce reputation and plenty of national pride. The “Ayatollah” celebration from the home fans is one of British football’s most distinctive sights. South Wales derbies between Cardiff and Swansea give the ground a hostile edge few other rivalries can match.

The world’s oldest international stadium still in use, having hosted Wales in 1877. The dramatic rise of Wrexham has turned it into a sold-out, rocking ground for nearly every game. Its compact old stands and the global spotlight create a nice buzz in and around the ground on a matchday.

The “Green and White Army” have made it one of the most intimidating international venues in Europe for its size, as Northern Ireland's only inclusion in the list from AI.

Bigger nations over the years have regularly found Belfast a miserable place to play, and the home crowd can take plenty of credit for that on the international stage, as well as the success that Linfield supporters have there.

“Can they do it on a cold, rainy night at Stoke?” became English football shorthand for a hostile test. The ground’s exposed site means wind swirls in, and the crowd’s “Delilah” anthem is roared at full volume.

Aberdeen are the first Scottish club mentioned by AI, as they're in at number 21 on the list for Britain's most intimidating football stadiums, which isn't too surprising.

They're not quite at the level of the so-called 'big boys' in the Scottish top-flight, but they're a more than worthy inclusion on the list.

The away entrance is famously through a row of terraced houses, which immediately throws visitors off. The stands are tight to the pitch and the crowd is right on top of players. Few grounds feel so cramped, so old and so hostile in such a small space when Luton Town are at their best.

Burnley have played here since 1883, and the ground’s old-school feel and Lancashire weather make it famously hard work. The Burnley supporters haven't had too much to cheer in recent years, and they'll be eager to see their side so much-needed improvement at the earliest of opportunities.

A compact, modern stadium that sits close to the pitch and amplifies the crowd. “Sunshine on Leith” belted out by the home end is one of Scottish football’s great sights and sounds. The Edinburgh derby and the fanatical home support make it a tough trip for visiting teams in the Scottish Premiership.

The South Stand, with around 17,500 people, is the largest single-tier stand in Britain and a sheer wall of fans. It was designed specifically to channel noise towards the pitch. At full voice on European nights, it’s as imposing as any modern stadium in Europe, and Roberto De Zerbi will be eager to see supporters firing up their team if they're at their best.

The new ground at Bramley-Moore Dock was designed to recreate Goodison Park's intensity, which was always going to be a tough ask.

The roof and the stands’ proximity to the pitch holds the noise in, and with a fanbase that are keen to see their side build on a strong start to this year's Premier League campaign, The Hill Dickinson Stadium will be a tough one to visit this term and moving forward.

I tested the brand-new Lavazza Tablì — it's better than Nespresso and Keurig and ten times easier than traditional manual espresso machines, making it perfect for coffee beginners

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock and manage exclusive Tom’s Guide member rewards.

Join the Tom's Guide Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Lavazza Tablì is one of the most exciting, fun coffee products I’ve tested this year. Not only does it look cool, it’s also extremely easy to use and makes delicious espresso. Its coffee is tastier and better for the environment than Nespresso (and, obviously, Keurig). I just wish there were a few more flavors available, but this could change in time.

Only shot glasses (or small mugs) fit under spout

Why you can trust Tom's Guide Our writers and editors spend hours analyzing and reviewing products, services, and apps to help find what's best for you. Find out more about how we test, analyze, and rate.

Pod espresso machines are often reviled by serious coffee snobs. Nespresso, Keurig, and everything in between — it’s just not “real coffee”. Their words, not mine. But the Lavazza Tablì is the closest to “real coffee” a pod machine has ever got.

I’ve not been this excited about a coffee machine in a while. The Tablì doesn’t use plastic-encased or aluminum-bound pods, but 100% coffee “Tabs”. It’s as pure as you can get. At first, I wondered if this was more a gimmick than an actual innovation, but after testing the Tablì for over a week (and using up all my Tabs), I have to say: the Lavazza Tablì is seriously special.

Is this enough to make the Lavazza Tablì one of the best espresso machines? Yes, without a doubt. I’m a tough crowd, but the Tablì has done the impossible: foolproof usability, affordability, and genuinely lip-smacking coffee. It’s a home run. Still not convinced? Find out the full story in this Lavazza Tablì review.

The Lavazza Tablì is available in a few different variants. The machine alone costs $129 from Amazon U.S. and £199 from John Lewis in the U.K, which is incredibly affordable (especially in the U.S., where list prices are typically higher than in Britain). When I saw the U.S. price, I actually gasped.

You can also buy the Tablì with a Lavazza milk frother (which is the version Lavazza sent me). This will set you back $199 from Amazon U.S. You can’t buy this bundle in the U.K., but the milk frother is an extra £79 from John Lewis, making it £278 overall. I’m unsure why it’s so much more expensive in the U.K., but it is what it is.

In the U.S., you can also get the full set: Tablì, milk frother, and 60 Tabs for $249 from Amazon U.S. A similar variant in the U.K. is: Tablì, milk frother, and 48 Tabs for £199 — but only directly from Lavazza.

I will do my very best to explain just how affordable this is. $129 for the Tablì is fantastic. I was honestly expecting $200-$250 when I unboxed it. Similar Nespresso machines would be the CitiZ or Pixie, $319 and $179, respectively. Add the Nespresso Aeroccino on top of that and you’re looking at an extra $100.

Get instant access to breaking news, the hottest reviews, great deals and helpful tips.

True, Nespresso Vertuo machines are cheaper: you’ve got the $129 Nespresso Vertuo Pop+ and the $229 Nespresso Vertuo Up. Still, the $100 price difference between the Tablì and Vertuo Up might not be enough to justify the latter’s ice mode if you’re on a tight budget.

The Lavazza Tablì is gorgeous. While it’s quite large — 15 inches deep and 13 inches tall — nothing about its design suggests “bulky”. This is a modern, stylish piece of tech that’ll elicit some interested questions from houseguests. I can’t count the times my colleagues directed “Oooh” or “Aaah” sounds towards it. Of all the coffee machines I’ve tested, this received the most interested comments — matched only by the beautiful Lelit MaraX3.

On the machine itself are four buttons: one for power, one for a double shot, one for a single shot, and one for a long shot.

Three lights on top of the machine correspond to the water tank, grounds drawer, and cleaning. These light up should you need to refill the tank, empty the drawer, or run a cleaning cycle.

There’s nothing confusing about the Tablì. It’s a straightforward, simple machine that doesn’t skimp on aesthetics or charisma.

The sole negative is the small space between the pouring spout and drip tray. You must use a shot glass or small mug as your receptacle; normal-sized mugs will not fit.

I’ll quickly mention the Tablì app. Yes, there’s an app. No, you don’t need to download it. I would recommend avoiding the app — not because it’s horrendous, but because it’s completely superfluous.

You can make espresso recipes, but because this is literally just a pod coffee machine, the only thing you can do is adjust temperature and volume. You can save said recipes, as you can see in my screenshots, and you can send these recipes to the machine. At first, I thought the app was glitchy, but it just takes a bit of experimenting to find out how to actually navigate it. Of course, you can buy Tabs in the app, which is probably the only genuinely useful feature.

I’d skip the app unless you’re a smart home fiend.

Now onto the good stuff: taste. As mentioned earlier, the Tablì is extremely easy to use. All you do is slide up the top, insert the Tab, and press the brew button. That’s it. The shots come out as two single shots or one double, which you can control by flipping up the pouring spout (single) or leaving it down (double).

At the time of writing, you can get five flavors of coffee: espresso intenso, qualita rossa, qualita oro, ristretto, and decaf. Of the bunch, my favorite was oro. I’d like to see Lavazza introduce some new flavors in time: ideally, some kind of juicy, fruity African bean. Those who’ve been here long enough know that I much prefer a lighter-roasted Ethiopian coffee to a dark-roasted Brazilian coffee. Obviously, this is just personal preference, and if you like those rich, full-bodied, chocolatey espressos, you’re the perfect candidate for Lavazza’s default beans anyway.

I do wonder if, assuming the Tablì (rightly) takes off, we’ll start seeing some third-party “Tabs”. Or if some coffee whiz will invent a way to make your own tabs. Or if Lavazza will introduce a Tab-making product itself, so you could theoretically grind your own beans, then compact them into a tab. However, that’s a lot of effort, and the Tablì isn’t really a lots-of-effort type of machine.

As for the coffee itself, well, it tastes so much better than any other pod machine I’ve tried. Although it’s definitely over-pressurized espresso — you can tell by how aerated and fluffy the crema is — it tastes wonderful. There’s zero bitterness whatsoever, and a well-rounded, strong coffee flavor.

I tested the Lavazza Tablì and the $1,999 Philips Cafe Aromis 8000 Series back to back, and which one tasted better? The Tablì, and boy, it wasn’t even close.

The prevailing issue holding the Tablì back — right now — is the lack of variety in the pods. Sorry, Tabs. Nespresso makes a huge variety of flavors, using every type of bean from around the world, and even has special Christmas, fall, and summer flavors. If you’re the kind of coffee drinker who jumps at the chance to try maple praline or pumpkin spice-flavored pods, then I might recommend sticking with Nespresso.

However, it’s still early days for the Tablì, and you never know what Lavazza might be cooking up behind the scenes.

Even though it doesn’t come by default, I’ll quickly discuss the milk frother. If you get the Tablì with the milk frother, you won’t be disappointed. Here’s a photo of the gorgeous milk texture.

As you can see, the bubbles are velvety and small, with no textural unevenness. While I can’t classify this as an explicit “Pro” — the Tablì doesn’t come with the milk frother — I would recommend grabbing the milk frother too if you like milk drinks. You don’t even have to use it with the Tablì; you could make hot chocolates or cold foam.

As I’ve mentioned, the Tablì isn’t exactly a compact machine. The Nespresso CitiZ is relatively smaller, at 11 x 12 inches compared to the Tablì’s 15 x 13. If you need a seriously small coffee maker, I might suggest the Nespresso instead.

However, the Tablì wins in every other contest. It’s prettier, tastes so much better, and is better for the environment. Nespresso pods can be recycled for free, which is better than Keurig charging $10 to recycle 96 pods (I still can’t believe that; it’s horrifying). However, manufacturing and recycling aluminum pods still generates waste and uses resources.

The Tablì’s Tabs are 100% coffee. No aluminum wrapping, no plastic casing. You can literally take the pucks out of the machine and throw them on the garden compost, or in your food waste. They’re just coffee. Of course, the Tabs come in a little cardboard box — that looks like a little chocolate box — but everything comes in a package. Whole coffee beans come in plastic bags.

Tabs cost around $1 a pop. This is par for the course with pod machines — Nespresso pods will set you back around $0.90 each, and Vertuo pods are around $1.40 (flavour dependent).

As the water tank is filled from above, you’ll need a decent amount of clearance space. However, like every other coffee machine I’ve ever reviewed, the grounds drawer is removed from the front.

Lavazza offers a 2-year warranty, which is the same as Nespresso, De’Longhi, Breville, and Ninja.

I’m sure you can tell from this review, but I adore the Lavazza Tablì. I’ve not been this excited by a coffee machine since I tested the Lelit MaraX3 earlier in the year. I’ve never been this excited by a pod machine.

And it’s just $129? How? How is this possible? $129 for a machine capable of producing this quality of espresso is unheard of. Sure, Nespresso Vertuo machines can be found for around $129, too, but none taste as good as the Tablì. Vertuo pods are extracted by centrifugal force, while Tablì uses traditional espresso pressure (espresso literally means pressure in Italian).

Want a pod machine that makes actually delicious espresso? The Lavazza Tablì is for you. I can’t wait to see how this innovation transforms the coffee machine world.

Erin Bashford is a senior writer at Tom's Guide, focusing on reviews. She has a Masters in Broadcast and Digital Journalism from the University of East Anglia. As an ex-barista, she knows her way around a coffee machine, and as a music lover, she's constantly chipping away at her dream of having a multi-room home sound system. In her spare time you can find her reading, practising yoga, writing, or stressing over today’s NYT Games.

Perché al Milan conviene uscire dall’Europa League per il campionato. La lezione di Amorim: “Fermarsi al derby è da piccola squadra”

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Può sembrare una provocazione e invece si tratta di una brutale lezione di realismo. Se fossi nel Milan lascerei perdere l’Europa League. E non solo perché le statistiche, nel calcio, non mentono. Anzi raccontano la verità. E documentano che mai una squadra, sia pure attrezzata per competere sui due fronti, e il Milan attuale non lo è certamente, è riuscita a fare strada in Europa league senza compromettere il cammino in campionato. Ci sono solo due esempi che portano a questa conclusione. Entrambi appartengono alla carriera di Antonio Conte. La prima volta, con la Juve lanciata verso lo scudetto dei record e dei 102 punti, preferì privilegiare quel dato e perse invece la grande occasione di poter disputare la finale di Europa League nella città di Torino. La seconda volta avvenne molto altro. Perché con l’Inter, Conte riuscì nell’estate del covid, a raggiungere la finale di Europa League poi persa ma nel frattempo era arrivato al secondo posto. A Gasperini con l’Atalanta è riuscita l’impresa continentale. Se giochi di giovedì e poi sempre di domenica, come capita al Milan, allora è difficile reggere sul doppio fronte.

Anche perché nel frattempo la lista degli infortunati è diventata sempre più lunga. Adesso Chukwueze si è operato da uno specialista all’anca e il suo rientro è stimato intorno ai tre mesi. Unitamente ai lungo degenti Gabbia, Tomori e Saelemaekers, concentrati in un solo ruolo tutti gli assenti mi sembra un dato che non può essere certo colmato prima di gennaio. Ecco perché sostengo che il Milan deve rinunciare all’Europa league.

C’è un altro motivo e provo a riepilogare qualche puntata precedente. Quale credete sia stata la differenza tra il Milan esaltante visto nel derby sabato notte e quello deprimente invece segnalato contro il Bologna qualche giorno prima? Pensate davvero che le assenze degli 8-9 infortunati (risparmiati compresi) non abbia inciso? O pensate per caso che l’infortunio di Tomori non abbia complicato visibilmente la tenuta difensiva già precaria dei suoi? La mia risposta non può che essere una soltanto: il Milan può dedicarsi solo a un obiettivo.

Amorim, nella conferenza di mercoledì all’ora di pranzo, mi è molto piaciuto perché ha pronunciato un paio di frasi che devono essere scolpite e affisse nello spogliatoio di Milanello. “Le squadre che si dedicano solo alle grandi sfide sono piccole squadre!”. È verissimo. Ed è il grande errore commesso in passato da quei gruppi che avevano l’ambizione a vincere per censo ma che non inseguivano il successo se non saltuariamente, scegliendosi qualche partita da vincere per poi vivere di rendita durante il resto della stagione. E un derby non può consentire al Milan di vivere di rendita per una stagione. Ma bisogna anche, contestualmente, essere consapevoli dei propri limiti e delle proprie difficoltà. Se il Milan è quello schierato nei primi 60-70 minuti del derby ha un certo futuro, se invece deve far ricorso, come contro il Bologna, alla sua panchina allora l’obiettivo deve essere ridimensionato. Lo so: ci vuole un coraggio da leone per patrocinare una tesi del genere. Ma credo che sia meglio raccontare la verità piuttosto che nasconderla per non apparire cinici.

Crédit Mutuel Arkéa resumes operations after attack, assesses losses

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The bank said customer funds were unaffected after attackers accessed its systems through a service provider’s platform.

French banking group Crédit Mutuel Arkéa said nearly all operations resumed Wednesday after a cyberattack linked to a suspected theft from the bank’s own account.

The bank said operations resumed following security checks and additional safeguards. It said customer funds were unaffected and the disruption only delayed certain transfers.

The bank also disclosed that attackers gained access through a service provider, not its online banking subsidiary Fortuneo. They transferred the bank’s money to other financial institutions, potentially allowing them to convert it into crypto.

Withdrawals from crypto exchanges first exposed the suspected theft. Blockchain analytics firm Bitquery traced withdrawals from accounts at Kraken, Crypto.com and OKX before the victim was publicly identified.

Onchain sleuth ZachXBT flagged more than $106 million in suspicious withdrawals on Monday and raised his estimate above $133 million on Tuesday after finding another $27 million. His Wednesday report identified Arkéa as the potential victim.

The bank then confirmed the attack and said it was still assessing its financial impact.

“We are not confirming these figures; we can only confirm the approximate scale,” Arkéa said, according to a machine translation.

Arkéa said some funds had been frozen and further recovery efforts were underway. Kraken and Crypto.com previously told Cointelegraph they had restricted accounts or blocked funds linked to the suspected theft. Both exchanges maintained that their platforms remained secure.

Xinhua Silk Road: Smart auto factory of Xiaomi offers sample of advanced manufacturing in China

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BEIJING, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- In early November, a bustling intelligent factory of Xiaomi in southeast Beijing is brimming with modernity and vitality when automated assembly robots are busy helping new vehicles roll off production lines.

Despite being a common sight for its staff, the frequently visited Xiaomi auto factory located in Beijing Economic-Technological Development Area represents a splendid sample for international visitors to observe smart manufacturing in China.

On November 5, the 7th World Media Summit kicked off in Beijing and drew crowds of guests from mainstream media outlets, think tanks and public departments across 112 countries and regions, as well as international organizations.

Scheduled to tour the factory, these guests are expected to discover that the smart auto plant stands not only as a manufacturing base of Xiaomi, but also as a miniature of China's rapidly-evolving NEV industry.

Covering an area of approximately 718,000 square meters, the Xiaomi auto factory is an integrated smart manufacturing campus that brings together NEV R&D, production, sales and customer experience.

Generally speaking, the site includes an R&D and testing base, six workshops dedicated to NEV production, a 2.5-kilometer-long testing track, and the Xiaomi auto factory store.

With traditional auto-making processes upgraded into six specialized procedures for NEVs, the Xiaomi auto factory shows how over 700 industrial robots work in tandem to streamline the production steps.

Adopting pioneering AI quality inspection technologies, the smart auto factory is delivering NEVs with supreme quality consistency and quality control precision far better than that of manual workers.

Being a typical representative of China's NEV industry and intelligent manufacturing, the Xiaomi auto factory has aroused broad attention at home and abroad as it demonstrates not only China's emerging auto culture but also strengths in auto technologies.

For the factory, however, advanced manufacturing is not limited to efficiency. On its factory roofs, rows of solar panels well interpret its green development philosophy as they generate electricity whose carbon emission reduction equals the total carbon dioxide absorbed by 540,000 trees in a year.

With models from its range-extended SUV series SkyNomad designated as the summit's vehicles, Xiaomi has delivered over 800,000 vehicles in total by the end of October, serving as a proactive participant in China's manufacturing upgrade.

Where AI thinks Celtic, Rangers & Hearts would finish in the EFL Championship

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There are a lot of debates that take place throughout the football season, and one of the most common centres on how Scottish clubs would compete in England.

As we know, Celtic and Rangers are two of the biggest clubs in Britain when it comes to the size of their support, as well as their history.

So, there has often been a sense of intrigue as to how the Glasgow giants would cope in English football if they were given a chance, with many contrasting opinions on just how they would do.

FLW ranks the top 10 sleeping giants in British football right now.

And, with Hearts having split the Old Firm last season, as they narrowly missed out on the title on a dramatic final day at Celtic Park, it would only be fair to include the Jambos in the discussion.

With that in mind, we asked AI to predict how the three top clubs in Scotland right now would do if they were in the 2026/27 Championship with their current squads…

Celtic do lead the way in Scotland at the moment, but it’s fair to say that it’s not a happy camp under Martin O’Neill, with the team on a wretched run by their high standards.

There has been plenty of anger directed towards the hierarchy from the fans, who are understandably furious at the lack of ambition and planning, whilst their European form remains very poor, even after missing out on the Champions League.

Nevertheless, a recent 7-1 hammering of Hearts is proof that there is still quality in the Celtic squad, and, given their pedigree, it’s perhaps not a surprise that AI thinks they would compete for automatic promotion in the Championship with the likes of Middlesbrough, West Ham and Wolves.

They ultimately predicted a 2nd-placed finish for the Hoops, which may be up for debate when you look at just how much the Hammers and Wolves spent on their respective squads compared to Celtic.

Rangers don’t have the recent history of their bitter rivals, as the only title they’ve won since 2011 was in the Covid season, but they will fancy their chances of winning the league this season.

But, Rangers still need to prove themselves as the best team in Scotland, so it’s understandable that AI states they are below Celtic at the moment.

Where do you think Rangers would finish in the Championship? Let us know in the comments below.

A 5th-placed finish was the prediction for Rangers if they were in England’s second tier, and most would agree that they have enough quality to be competing for promotion, as there has been serious investment into the squad from their US owners, who know all about the Championship due to their links with Leeds United.

There was a lot of change at Ibrox in the summer following the appointment of Derek McInnes from Hearts, and their embarrassing failure to reach the group stage in a European competition shows there is still work to be done. But, Rangers are heading in the right direction, and they would fancy their chances of reaching the play-offs at least.

As mentioned, even though they fell short at the last minute, last season was a memorable one for Hearts, who deserved so much credit for how they competed against teams with more resources than them.

That success came at a cost though, as Rangers took top scorer Lawrence Shankland and key midfielder Cammy Devlin as well as McInnes, despite finishing above them.

With minority investor Tony Bloom and his Jamestown Analytics company helping with recruitment, Hearts have brought in quality to replace those who moved on, and they will feel they still have a good squad.

However, it’s no surprise AI ranks them below their Scottish counterparts, and it’s said the Edinburgh outfit would be competing for the play-offs, although they suggested they’d end up outside the top eight, as they went for an 11th-placed finish.

Given the financial power of many Championship clubs, that would be a fair position for Hearts, and, with Bloom’s guidance, they would back themselves to make up that gap very quickly - just as they plan to do north of the border.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Prime Infra’s CEO says the Paris 1.5-degree goal is ‘feasible’ for Southeast Asia—if governments are willing to pay for it

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Southeast Asia can still meet the Paris Agreement’s most ambitious climate goal—but only if governments accept higher energy costs, the CEO of one of the Philippines’ biggest infrastructure developers said on Tuesday.

“I do think it’s feasible, but I think it will require sacrificing cost to achieve the objective,” said Guillaume Lucci, president and CEO of Prime Infrastructure Capital, onstage at Fortune’s Southeast Asia 500 Summit in Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam.

“That will be a very difficult decision for a lot of the governments in our region to decide if we drive cost up and decarbonize, or maybe not so, and rethink some of these targets,” Lucci added, calling himself “a bit of a pessimist” on the question of whether the region can power an AI-era growth spurt without breaking its decarbonization targets.

Under the Paris Agreement, countries committed to holding the rise in global average temperature to well below 2°C above preindustrial levels and pursuing efforts to limit it to 1.5°C.

The Philippines, where Prime Infrastructure Capital is based, imports 98% of its oil from the Middle East. The Southeast Asian country declared a nationwide energy emergency in March, sending local fuel prices soaring.

The country’s grid is not ready for the energy transition, either. “We have fairly poor grids [and] certainly not grids that are capable of receiving an abundance of variable energy,” Lucci said. “We’re building 2,000 megawatts of pumped hydro right now that will help stabilize the grid in the Philippines.”

Lucci’s caution is rooted in the Philippines, which imports 98% of its oil from the Gulf and declared a nationwide energy emergency in March after the Iran war broke out in February, sending local fuel prices soaring.

Kim-See Lim, chief investment officer of the Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank (AIIB), struck a more optimistic note, pointing to the region’s young, tech-savvy population and its rapid growth over the past two decades.

“It’s been the fastest-growing region in the last two decades, projected to continue on that trajectory,” Lim said during the panel. She added that Southeast Asia will need to transition because the effects of climate change are “felt quite severely” in the region.

About 25% of the world’s seaborne oil trade went through the Strait of Hormuz in 2025, and an estimated 80% of the oil and oil products passing through the strait were destined for Asia, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). “The LNG prices are still double of where it used to be before the Middle East issue, and oil prices are close to double as well,” Lim noted, citing the energy shock that followed the war.

Asked about the region’s energy investment gap, Lim said the main obstacle was not capital but a shortage of investor confidence in projects. Companies still expect a minimum return in emerging markets, she said, while perceived risk remains “too high.”

“Clearly, capital is not lacking,” she said. “What’s lacking really is the confidence, right? And that’s bankable projects.”

The IEA projects Southeast Asia’s electricity demand will rise by a further 5.4% annually between 2026 and 2030.

The disruption matters sharply for Asia. About 25% of the world’s seaborne oil trade went through the Strait of Hormuz in 2025, and options for oil flows to bypass Hormuz are limited, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). An estimated 80% of oil and oil products that went through the Strait of Hormuz were destined for Asia.

Southeast Asia’s next growth cycle will depend on access to secure, affordable, and reliable energy. The IEA reports that regional electricity demand is projected to rise by a further 5.4% annually during 2026 to 2030.

Data centers are putting pressure on energy systems. Global electricity consumption from data centers reached an estimated 415 terawatt hours in 2024—about 1.5% of global electricity consumption—and the IEA says AI is accelerating deployment of high-performance servers, driving up power density in data centers.

“Many of the data centers have to move far from the community to the energy park,” said Xingjian Pang, Schneider Electric’s East Asia president. Locating facilities near communities could provoke protests over their electricity and water use, he said.

Netflix might want its new AI search to drum up more “quality” viewership

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Netflix‘s new strategy for combating YouTube‘s commanding share of living room TVs is to insist that it’s not the quantity of viewership, but the quality.

And it might be trying to pump up those quality views with a new recommendation tool.

Of course, the times being what they are, the tool is AI.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

AI Discover is intended to curate Netflix’s offerings in a hyperpersonalized manner, so people only see what they like. If this sounds familiar, it’s because Netflix is doing what YouTube, X, and Meta have already done: shunt the responsibility for a quality recommendation algorithm onto viewers and their input.

“There’s more to enjoy and discover on Netflix than ever, but what sounds right for a quiet night in may look different for family movie night or a few spare minutes,” Netflix said in an announcement post. “We’ve spent years refining how you discover, with recommendations tailored to your tastes, from what you Thumbs Up to what you add to ‘My List.’ Now, AI Discover joins the mix as a new way to explore.”

Once a theme is in place, Netflix’s bot brings up a bunch of suggestions, and also additional narrower themes within the first themes. Netflix says there will be a process of “tuning” AI Discover’s recommendations until it “find[s] your next obsession.”

Users who think the bot is getting too far off what they want can refresh the whole deal. Netflix also lets people tell it titles they don’t like.

Unlike the aforementioned social media platforms, Netflix doesn’t seem to have included a ChatGPT-esque discussion aspect to this tool. Users just click through and select what they like, and what they don’t.

Makes sense, since YouTube, Meta, and X have their own in-house chatbots they can shove into everything; Netflix doesn’t have a Gemini or Grok or Muse equivalent, so a yes/no system is its best option. It did partner with OpenAI in 2025 to test an AI search feature that was rumored would replace its regular search function, but that never materialized in full across the service. Maybe this is the version of that idea it ultimately ended up with, sans GPT chatting.

Netflix does say AI Discover is “powered by a new intelligence layer we’ve been building that responds to what members tell us in real time and keeps learning.”

This brings us back to what we said at the top of this article: Netflix is looking for quality viewership. And, well, if it can gather an immense amount of information about viewers’ preferences with this new “intelligence layer,” it might be able to more accurately match them with what they want to watch.

That in turn could drive up the amount of time people spend watching a movie or a TV show–and Netflix might start shooting for average view duration as a way to supercede YouTube.

For now, Netflix’s new AI discovery is only available on TVs in the U.S. (interesting choice), and only in English. Netflix says it won’t appear on kids’ profiles. Per Variety, the streamer plans to expand to more international users in the coming weeks.

Milan-Ferencvaros, quote marcatore

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Niente più passi falsi. Il Milan, dopo aver perso 2 partite su 3 in Europa League deve vincere a tutti i costi contro il Ferencvaros, per cercare di arrivare tra le prime otto in classifica ed evitare un turno extra a febbraio. Attenzione agli ungheresi che hanno vinto fin qui tutte le partite della League Phase. Appuntamento alle 18.45 a San Siro.

7 reti segnate in 11 partite, media di un gol ogni 79'. Alphadjo Cisse è la ventata di freschezza in un Milan in trasformazione. L'unico successo raccolto dai rossoneri in Europa League lo ha visto come grande protagonista: la sua tripletta a Salisburgo lo ha resto l'italiano più giovane a portarsi a casa il pallone in una competizione europea. Contro il Ferencvaros è probabile vederlo dal primo minuto, i suoi inserimenti dalla trequarti hanno fin qui fatto malissimo.

Prima di procedere con il seconda pronostico sul match, dai un'occhiata alle quote +EV elaborate dalla nostra Intelligenza Artificiale. Il software, stimando la probabilità reale di ogni evento, segnala automaticamente quando un bookmaker sottovaluta un esito, evidenziando le opportunità a Valore Atteso Positivo.

Nella tabella TOP +EV BETS che vedi di seguito troverai le migliori proposte in tempo reale sulla gara. Ma come si leggono queste statistiche? Ora te lo spiego:

* Giocata consigliata: sulla sinistra trovi subito il mercato consigliato, con subito sotto anche tutti i dati sul tipo di competizione e le info su partita, data e orario; * Migliore Quota: sulla destra trovi la miglior quota presente sul mercato in quel momento sulla giocata consigliata; * EV: subito sotto la quota trovi anche il Valore Atteso Positivo sul mercato, in percentuale.

Sprecata l'occasione contro il Salisburgo, Francesco Camarda è finito in panchina a Bournemouth. Soli 96 minuti giocati fin qui e zero reti. Di questi tempi, due anni fa, faceva il suo esordio europeo nella sfida contro il Brugge. Il suo gol fece esplodere San Siro. Rete poi annullata per fuorigioco. Da allora il talento cerca ancora il primo centro con la prima squadra del Milan. Che sia questa la grande notte?

What the Democrats' midterms win means for the US economy and Trump

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From high oil prices and data centers to tariffs and mounting debt, economic concerns played a key role in the crushing defeat of President Donald Trump's Republicans. So can the Democrats now make a difference?

For the millions who cast their ballots in the US midterm elections, one issue ranked above all others: the economy.

So what does the Democrats' stunning capture of both the US Senate and House of Representatives mean for the economy and economic policy, given that President Donald Trump still has two years of his term to go?

Markets have rebounded after initially trending negatively. Global stocks rallied as investors appeared to take heart from the Democrats' victory.

With Democrats now holding comfortable majorities in both houses of Congress and Trump still in the White House, it could lead to gridlock in Washington. Trump has already said he would become "Mr Veto," meaning he would block any legislation the Democrats advance.

"The magnitude of the gains for the Democrats in the midterms does not change too much for the near-term policy outlook, given the party still lacks a filibuster-proof majority in the Senate and President Trump has veto power to block legislation," Stephen Brown, chief North America economist with Capital Economics, said in a note.

"I think we are looking at two years of complete 100% gridlock," Jacob Funk Kirkegaard, nonresident senior fellow with the Peterson Institute for International Economics, told DW after the results came in. "Nothing will get done of any kind."

Some investors appear worried that the Democrats could force through AI regulation, especially if they also win the White House in 2028. Rising opposition among large majorities of the US population to data centers — the key infrastructure behind AI — helped secure victories for many Democratic candidates.

Kirkegaard, who is also a senior fellow at Bruegel, a European economics think tank, said the vote showed the extent of that opposition.  Even if the Democrats can't force regulation, he thinks local opposition will make it harder for data centers to be built, which could hit the valuation of big tech firms that have benefitted from the AI boom.

"This election shows that the political underpinning of the rapid, aggressive AI growth story is shaky," he said. "It doesn't mean it's going to stop or be made illegal, but the dynamic of the growth story has shifted."

If the Democrats were to win the presidency in 2028, Brown said, they would be perfectly placed to make major policy changes. "That includes the potential for tax hikes on corporations and high-income individuals as well as regulation that could delay the AI rollout," he said.

According to polling conducted in early October by the Pew Research Center, 56% of Americans say Trump's tariff policies will have mostly negative impacts on the country, with only 20% saying mostly positive.

That sentiment clearly drove many Democrats to victory in the midterms — particularly in states where tariffs have had a particularly negative impact. That includes those with sectors reliant on exports, such as Texas, Michigan, Iowa and Illinois, where counter-tariffs have hit and those that have high trade volumes with Canada.

Yet, barring an unprecedented climbdown from Trump — who has been ideologically wedded to tariffs since he first became a public figure almost 50 years ago — it's not clear how the Democrats could force substantial change to the president's signature economic policy.

"Trump will not be moderated," Deborah Elms, head of trade policy at the Hinrich Foundation in Singapore, told DW earlier this year. "Expect him to continue his trade disruption and tariff policies for as long as he remains in the Oval Office."

Though the US Constitution gives Congress the power to collect and organize tariffs, various laws passed over the years have given presidents power to implement tariffs when they cite certain conditions, such as national security or economic emergencies.

Trump has already used such avenues for his tariffs and can continue to do so, despite some legal rebukes. However, while Congress can't necessarily force an end to Trump's existing tariffs, it can now take further legal action against them to prevent greater escalation.

Though Trump and the Democrats are clearly not going to make common cause, Kirkegaard said, the scale of the Republicans' defeat might force Trump to be less erratic in his policymaking, from escalating his trade war with Canada to his Iran policy.

Trump said in the aftermath of Tuesday's defeats that he would "work with" the Democrats and told the Financial Times in the run-up to the vote that he was unlikely to escalate the war in Iran.

Were a deal to be struck with Iran, soaring energy prices — a pivotal election issue — could finally fall, helping voters with their cost-of-living concerns.

"You've got to get a deal with Iran, and you've got to bring down gas prices," Kirkegaard said. "From a political point of view, that's the most important thing now."

George Saravelos, an analyst with Deutsche Bank FX Research, thinks the markets are beginning to become more optimistic in that area. "With oil flows through the Middle East having materially recovered, the market is pricing a large imminent risk premium of re-escalation and energy supply disruption," he said in a note.

Despite the scale of the Democrats' victory, the fact that Trump remains in the Oval Office will complicate all efforts to force a change to signature economic policies and issues.

Yet Congress can now exert considerable pressure. Another issue vital to voters was corruption and the belief that the Trump administration has engaged in corruption and personal enrichment while in office.

That raises the prospect of the new Democratic-controlled Congress launching legal investigations and potentially pursuing another impeachment of Trump, which could force the president to negotiate on some economic issues to secure his political survival.

"Can you find 10 or 12 Republican senators who, in the face of overwhelming corruption, would be willing to vote for articles of impeachment? I wouldn't rule it out," Kirkegaard said. "And that's a scenario in which Trump might suddenly be much more accommodating to, for instance, abandoning most of his tariffs."

Despite voters' clear rejection of Trump's economic policies, the Democratic Party's newly minted senators, congressmen and congresswomen face an uphill battle in trying to reverse them.

The Reds lost their way after the teenager’s withdrawal in Sunday’s defeat to Arsenal as the midfielder shone on his first ever Premier League start

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Liverpool head coach Andoni Iraola grabbed Trey Nyoni and threw him in at the deep end. Sink or swim was the aim of the game. And the teenager kept his head above water, and then some.

The teenager made his first ever Premier League start on Sunday afternoon. The call was well deserved following a fine showing against Chelsea in the EFL Cup.

That it came against defending champions Arsenal was a huge show of faith in the midfielder. The Reds may have fallen to a narrow 1-0 home defeat to the Gunners, but Nyoni’s 59-minute display proved he’s ready for regular minutes in this Liverpool side.

You’d be forgiven for overlooking Nyoni’s showing at Anfield. Declan Rice, after all, was the star performer on Merseyside. Mikel Arteta’s ‘Master-of-all-Trades’ midfield man put in a dominant display in the middle of the park.

The assist for Kai Havertz’s one and only goal was the cherry on the icing atop the Arsenal cake.

Yet despite coming up against, arguably, one of the best midfielders in world football, Nyoni wasn’t overawed. Rather, he rose to the occasion to deliver a performance that perhaps convinced Iraola he is worthy of a starting spot at the expense of either Alexis Mac Allister or Ryan Gravenberch.

“I just tried to go out there and do what I do, tried to get on the ball as much as possible and bring the team forwards,” Nyoni told the official Liverpool website about making his debut in such a blockbuster event. Many would have been overwhelmed by the spotlight that came from such a moment. Nyoni, though, was buoyed on by the welcome of Arsenal.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

The 19-year-old ended the game with the best pass success rate (96.4%) of all starters in Sunday’s match. Only teammates Virgil van Dijk (67) and Mac Allister (61) made more passes than the academy product (56). The former pair played the full 90, whereas the latter was withdrawn on the hour mark.

Nyoni was dictating the tempo in the middle of the park, and driving Liverpool forward. Only Arsenal goalkeeper David Raya (46) made more forward passes than the youngster (24) at the weekend. Fourteen line-breaking passes was the most during his time on the pitch.

Iraola’s decision to withdraw Nyoni in place of the returning Dominik Szoboszlai meant that, for a 15-minute spell, Liverpool ceded momentum as they struggled to gain a foothold on the game.

From the 76th-minute onwards, they again dominated possession, even if it amounted to nothing, but Nyoni’s substitution saw the tie swing back in Arsenal’s favour at a crucial time in the clash. It’s within this 15-minute window where Arteta’s men begin to turn the screw in games. Only Manchester United (33) have had more shots than Arsenal (26) between minutes 60 and 75. Twenty-four chances created ranks top.

Liverpool needed a player who was comfortable in possession to ensure Arsenal were unable to build upon Havertz’s early strike. Of course, the momentum swing didn’t amount to another Gunners goal. Conversely, Liverpool would have enjoyed a more comfortable period of play had Nyoni remained on the pitch.

He brings some much-needed composure in a key area, a quality that had been found wanting following the departure of Curtis Jones. The sidelined Ryan Gravenberch brings the drive from deep, while Mac Allister offers the nitty-gritty, off the ball work to help protect the defence. Nyoni, meanwhile, delivers the forward thrust with his vision and distribution, qualities that far exceed both Gravenberch and Mac Allister, particularly in a 4-2-3-1 setup.

And having been dispossessed just four times in all competitions this season, Nyoni’s press resistance is another important trait. He’s comfortable receiving the ball from the centre-backs, before turning and setting his side on the frontfoot. Even under pressure, you’d back the up-and-coming Reds star to wriggle his way out of danger.

At his age, Nyoni, physically, still has some growing to do. Iraola can be expected to manage his minutes accordingly in order to avoid injury stunting his growth. Regardless, it’s clear that Nyoni is ready for a more prominent role in this Liverpool side.

Former boss Arne Slot used Nyoni sparingly, despite speaking highly of the player. In a difficult second, and final, season in the Anfield dugout, the Dutchman opted for experience. Injuries have perhaps forced Iraola’s hand this time around, but Nyoni has grabbed the opportunity to consolidate a starting spot with both hands.

A New UK Porn Ban Sparks Fears of Unprecedented Sex Censorship

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

A new UK law banning porn featuring choking and “fauxcest,” or simulated incest, may have unintended consequences for content creators and smut fans all over the world, some experts warn.

As of November 6, amendments to the Crime and Policing Act will outlaw the “possession or publication of pornographic images of strangulation or suffocation”—a law intended to curb violence against women and girls. Yet some experts warn that the legislation is written broadly enough that it could potentially criminalize far more innocuous content, including “breast smothering,” “jeans facesitting,” and even potentially oral sex, ostensibly because people could have their airways impeded during these activities.

“I would encourage both content creators and consumers to underscore the risks of this law,” says Myles Jackman, an obscenity lawyer in the UK. “Because to me, it genuinely seems like one of the greatest acts of suppression of free speech in global history.”

The new amendments were inspired by a lengthy pornography review authored by Baroness Gabby Bertin, a Conservative member of the UK House of Lords. The legislation bans various depictions of sex acts, including “pornographic images of strangulation or suffocation,” as well as content showing performers role-playing as minors under the age of 16.

It also takes aim at “fauxcest,” an extremely popular porn niche that depicts simulated sexual relations between family members who are not related by blood, such as stepsiblings or stepparents. In Bertin’s review recommending the ban on such content, she cites “anecdotal evidence” suggesting that fauxcest “can be used between an abuser and child to permit interfamily child sex abuse,” describing it as a “fig-leaf” of sorts for actual sexual abuse.

Under the new law, those caught distributing or in possession of such content face up to five years in prison and a fine.

The impending UK legislation has prompted panic among some adult creators, many of whom are currently combing through their content libraries and removing clips that could get swept up in the ban.

“The problem is always how broad [the legislation] is,” says Alexandra Snow, an adult content creator and educator. “And we won't know how they're going to interpret it until they start making people's lives hell.”

Snow says she is particularly concerned about how the aspect of the legislation pertaining to “suffocation or strangulation” will affect kink creators, who may make content related to collaring or leather fetishes. She’s also worried about AI image-recognition software, which is already in widespread use among the platforms, potentially flagging content that isn’t violative at all. “They use a very broad, heavy-handed approach,” she says.

Cami Strella, an OnlyFans creator in the US who estimates that 60 to 70 percent of her content depicts some form of role-play, including step-“fauxcest,” is also concerned about the legislation. She says that the content she makes is “just playing a fantasy,” and that more sex education and literacy is required to teach people about the risks of various behaviors.

“I don’t think banning it is going to do anything,” she says. “If anything, it’ll just push even worse content that is actually illegal or immoral underground, so you’ll have to access it away from these platforms. This is just a slippery slope we’re getting into.”

The new UK law is seemingly targeted at the trend of choking in porn, which has been linked to real-life instances of fatal choking during sex. Though the practice is highly risky, potentially leading to brain damage or death, it’s increasingly common among young adults, largely due to the prevalence of eroticized choking depictions in the media and in porn. The death of a 16-year-old Australian girl in August, who authorities say lost consciousness after she was choked consensually during sex, has led to renewed calls for legislation banning depictions of the practice in online porn, which has “absolutely sort of normalized this kind of sexual violence,” Bertin recently told the BBC.

The UK has a history of imposing stringent restrictions on internet pornography, particularly compared to the United States, where statutes like Section 230 of the Communications Decency Act of 1996 largely exempt platforms from liability for hosting objectionable content. Long before many US states started requiring users to verify their ages before visiting porn sites, for instance, the UK communications regulation agency Ofcom has been slapping websites with fines of up to 10 percent of their worldwide revenue for failing to comply with age-verification requirements.

“The UK is really pushing for limits on free speech,” says Michael Fattorosi, an adult industry attorney based in Italy. “This is just something they're throwing on top of the heap of restrictions.”

In a statement to WIRED, Clara Coleman, a spokesperson for the UK’s Ministry of Justice, denied that the legislation was intended to curb sexual expression. “We’re committed to halving violence against women and girls in a decade, and that includes preventing harm before it starts,” Coleman says. “[These] laws aren’t about policing people’s sexual freedom, they’re about cracking down on content that normalizes violence against women, encourages unlawful behavior, and can cause real-world harm.”

Jackman, the lawyer, says that he has no issue with the provision banning strangulation, as its risks are proven and there are existing laws banning strangulation in the UK. He cautions, however, that the law is written broadly enough that relatively innocuous content could also potentially be penalized.

“It would be up to a court to decide this on a case-by-case basis, but given that it would appear that face-sitting by a vagina owner is now arguably suffocation, the insertion of a penis could equally be considered suffocation because it blocks the airways,” he says. In other words, “the UK could have just banned blow jobs” in online pornography.

Though the law currently applies only to consumers and creators in the United Kingdom, the stiff penalties for “possession” of such images—which the law defines as an image being stored on a phone, a hard drive, or in cloud storage—is of particular concern to Fattorosi.

“As far as creators are concerned, if you visit the UK, if you’re going there on holiday, if you’re going there for a conference, you should probably be careful about what you keep on your phone,” he says.

The subscription-based platform OnlyFans is particularly vulnerable, he adds. Though most American porn companies don’t have a substantial presence in the UK, making it difficult for Ofcom to go after them, that doesn’t apply to OnlyFans. The subscription-based platform’s parent company, Fenix International Limited, is based in the United Kingdom and has already faced steep fines from the agency for failure to adhere to age-disclosure requirements.

“If I were OnlyFans,” Fattorosi says, “I’d be very worried about this.” (In a statement, OnlyFans tells WIRED the platform “complies with applicable laws and regulations in all jurisdictions in which it operates and has longstanding, proactive safety measures in place that meet UK legal requirements.”)

In advance of the new legislation taking effect, adult content platforms have issued updated guidelines for creators. The platform IWantClips has already started complying, publishing a new set of guidelines for creators and fans based in the UK and removing clips that could potentially be swept up in the legislation. The laundry list of sexual niches and categories banned now includes “breast smothering,” “ass smothering,” “jeans facesitting,” “BBW facesitting,” “plastic fetish,” “scissorhold,” and “butt drops.”

Trans Princess Jezebel, a dominatrix and adult content creator in the UK, says she has already started going through her content library on IWantClips and OnlyFans, removing videos that may be seen as violative. She’s heavily reliant on clips to advertise her services as a dominatrix, and she’s concerned that once the law takes effect on November 6, she’ll take a huge financial hit.

“When the state starts regulating which types of pornography people are allowed to watch, a dangerous precedent is set,” she tells WIRED in an email. “Where will it end?”

But even though the UK is, for the time being, an outlier, Jackman warns it may not be for long. He says he has heard of lawmakers in Italy, the Netherlands, and Spain discussing similar legislation, as well as members of the evangelical lobby in the United States.

“In terms of free speech,” he says, “the lights are going out across the globe.”

SC Ventures and SBI Join Soter Insure's Series B Alongside Follow-On from Token Bay Capital

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The strategic investment from two global institutional-grade players, alongside follow-on support from Token Bay Capital, joins a Galaxy-led round that also includes Coinbase Ventures, Franklin Templeton and Brevan Howard Digital.

ABU DHABI, UAE, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Soter Insure, a specialist insurer for the digital asset economy, today announced that SC Ventures, the innovation and ventures arm of Standard Chartered, and Japanese financial group SBI Group have joined its Series B as strategic investors, adding to the round led by Galaxy.

This second close also includes follow-on investment from Token Bay Capital, a Soter backer since its Series A. The new investors join Coinbase Ventures, Franklin Templeton and Brevan Howard Digital, which participated in the first close announced in July 2026.

SC Ventures and SBI join Soter Insure's Galaxy-led Series B, alongside follow-on from Token Bay Capital

Insurance capacity remains one of the main missing pieces for banks and asset managers entering digital assets. Without it, institutions cannot apply the same risk standards they use for every other asset class. The backing of two global financial groups that are active in digital assets reflects a growing recognition that specialized coverage is a prerequisite for institutional participation.

Soter Insure will use the proceeds to increase its insurance capacity, deepen its underwriting and technology capabilities, and expand into mature and emerging digital asset markets, including Asia-Pacific through its Hong Kong presence, Europe and the US.

"Banks can't scale in digital assets without the risk protection they rely on everywhere else, and today that protection is still scarce," said Henson Orser, Founder and CEO of Soter Insure. "With SC Ventures and SBI Group joining Galaxy, Coinbase Ventures and Franklin Templeton, we now have traditional finance and digital asset natives behind the same goal: insurance capacity at institutional scale. Our strategic investors now span most of the jurisdictions and networks at the forefront of digital asset adoption. We're grateful to Token Bay Capital for backing us again, and to Galaxy for leading the round."

Herve Francois, Principal, Digital Assets Fund, SC Ventures, said: "Insurance is a critical but often overlooked part of the digital assets ecosystem. Our investment reflects our belief that Soter is uniquely positioned to build a regulated insurance platform that can scale to meet institutional needs and support the industry's continued development globally."

Yangte Kim, Representative in the UAE of SBI Group, added: "As digital assets continue to gain broader adoption, we believe the development of robust insurance solutions will be an increasingly important part of the ecosystem. Soter brings together deep expertise across regulation, digital assets and traditional insurance to address this important market need. Through our investment, SBI Group looks forward to supporting Soter's growth and contributing to the continued development of a more resilient global digital asset ecosystem."

For more information about Soter Insure, visit soter.insure.

Headquartered in Abu Dhabi and operating out of Bermuda, with offices in London, New York and Dubai, Soter Insure was incubated by Further Ventures and WebN Group. The company offers specialized digital asset insurance products for financial institutions, including Directors & Officers (D&O), Professional Indemnity (PI), Crime, Specie, Slashing and Smart Contract Failure coverage, uniquely denominated in both fiat and native digital assets.

SC Ventures is Standard Chartered's innovation, fintech investment and venture-building platform. Since 2018, it has built, operated and scaled ventures, and made minority investments, deploying more than $1 billion across 30+ portfolio companies globally. It builds, invests in and partners with ventures shaping the future of financial services across digital banking, digital assets, SME growth, financial inclusion, trade and cross-border infrastructure. In the Middle East, SC Ventures has grown from 2 ventures to 10 in two years across the UAE and Saudi Arabia, with Egypt next on the roadmap, supporting regional growth through disciplined venture building, strategic partnerships and regulated innovation aligned with national economic visions.

Founded in 1999, the SBI Group is a comprehensive internet financial group and a pioneer of internet-based financial services in Japan. The Group operates a Financial Services Business centered on securities, banking, and insurance, providing highly convenient products and services at competitive prices through the internet. In addition, the SBI Group operates Asset Management Business, PE Investment Business, Crypto-asset Business, and Next Gen Business on a global scale.

Token Bay Capital is a venture capital firm investing in digital asset and blockchain infrastructure. Founded in Hong Kong in 2021 and now headquartered in Abu Dhabi Global Market (ADGM), Token Bay Capital Limited is authorized and regulated by the Financial Services Regulatory Authority (FSRA) as a Venture Capital Fund Manager. It backs early-stage companies building the infrastructure institutions need to use digital assets: custody, trading, payments, on-chain finance and tokenization.

Global Study: Canada Ranks Fifth Among 56 Commonwealth Countries for Youth AI Readiness, Yet Research from Ayre Think Tank Points to Gaps in Practical AI Use

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ayre Think Tank identifies potential C$100.85-billion AI-related GDP boost over the next decade though lower-scoring education and training provision could affect economic gains

ST JOHN'S, Antigua and Barbuda, Nov. 5, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Canada's strong connectivity and digital capabilities have placed it among the Commonwealth's leaders in youth AI readiness, but education and training provision emerges as a relative weakness, according to a comprehensive new international study by the Ayre Think Tank.

The inaugural Ayre Index ranked Canada fifth among 56 Commonwealth countries, behind Singapore, Malta, Australia and the United Kingdom, with an overall score of 70.2 out of 100. Canada scored 9.4 out of 10 for both meaningful access and digital, data, media and AI capability, compared with 5.3 for education and training delivery.

The findings were just released during the Commonwealth Heads of Government Meeting (CHOGM) in Antigua and Barbuda, attended by His Majesty King Charles III and Prime Minister Mark Carney.

Speaking about the Index, Calvin Ayre, Antiguan-Canadian philanthropist, entrepreneur and founder of the Ayre Think Tank, said: "I've spent my life in businesses where new technology changes everything, and I know you can't stand still. AI is going to change how we work, how we do business and how wealth is created. What matters is giving young people the skills and the chance to get ahead â whether to start businesses, find jobs or build something for themselves."

Ayre stressed that nobody can afford to be complacent right now. "Some Commonwealth countries are ahead, others have catching up to do. Nobody can afford to be complacent. That's why we created this Index. It shows countries where they stand, what they're getting right and where they need to do better. Governments now have to decide what they're going to do about it."

One key question facing Canada now is how it can convert its strong infrastructure and youth interest into practical capability-building among youth. The potential economic stakes are significant. Researchers applied International Monetary Fund (IMF) scenarios to Canada's economy and calculated that it could gain a potential AI-related GDP increase of C$100.85 billion over the next ten years under a moderate scenario. A conservative estimate is C$51.19 billion, and the optimistic case is C$150.44 billion.

"AI readiness is the minimum standard for future success. Canada needs to ensure its education systems equip young people to move beyond readiness and become AI fluent. While youth may be regular users of AI tools, the real priority is ensuring they know how to use AI effectively, question its outputs and harness its potential to build career skills and create opportunities," said Lisa Taylor, global authority on workforce systems and founder and CEO, Challenge Factory.

Developing AI fluency requires opportunities for informed, purposeful practice, yet, according to the Ayre Think Tank's analysis of OECD PISA 2025 data, only 37.7 per cent of Canadian 15-year-olds reported using AI chatbots for schoolwork at least weekly, compared with an OECD average of approximately 46 per cent. Separately, Statistics Canada's research found that just 10 per cent of employed 15- to 24-year-olds used generative AI at work over a 12-month period.

Moreover, the Index did not identify an explicit reference to AI in the teacher-policy evidence it used for Canada, drawing on UNESCO's country profile on technology in education. Together, these findings underscore the importance of strengthening AI education and practical skills development to better prepare young Canadians for an increasingly AI-driven workforce.

The Ayre Think Tank produced a white paper reporting on the findings from the Index, and the paper identifies five global priorities: affordable and reliable access to technology; education that develops practical skills; stronger routes from training into employment and enterprise; AI tools suited to local languages and needs; and safeguards that enable young people to question automated decisions.

It calls on governments, educators and development partners around the world to better understand the barriers young people face in each country before deciding where investment can make the greatest impact.

"We need to make sure that the next generation will be able to do more than just consume AI products made elsewhere; access must come with its own agency and a real equitable stake in how these systems are governed," said C. Justin Robinson, pro vice-chancellor and campus principal of the University of the West Indies, Five Islands Campus, who contributed to the white paper's foreword.

"Young people should be equipped to question new AI tools, to improve them and to be supported in building alternatives suited to their own needs â whether that happens, however, will depend on choices made now."

More about the Ayre Index and the Ayre Think Tank can be found at https://calvinayre.com/for-progress-think-tank.

📅 Nov 05, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The K-pop veterans will complete their 20th anniversary tour with a one-off show at SoFi Stadium next March.

BIGBANG will bring their world tour to a close with a newly-announced concert next March in Los Angeles.

The K-pop veterans add a one-off show at SoFi Stadium on Saturday, March 13, 2027, for what will be the final date on their extensive 20th anniversary run.

Since getting underway in August, BIGBANG 2026-2027 World Tour ‘XX : COSMOS’ has visited stadiums across Asia, North America, Europe, the United Kingdom and Australia, and is now winding its way through another pan-Asian route. Next up, the first of two nights at Rajamangala National Stadium in Bangkok, Thailand this Saturday, Nov. 7. Explore Bigbang See latest videos, charts and news

This tour sees G-Dragon, Taeyang and Daesung reunite for the first time since 2017, and for the first time as a three-piece. When the group played MetLife Stadium in September, the setlist ran through 30 songs across about three hours, Billboard’s Jeff Benjamin reported, and included “BiiiG,” BIGBANG’s first new single in four years. “BiiiG” dropped on Aug. 19 to mark 20 years to the day since the group’s debut. Chojo' as Vaundy's 'Shiwaawase' Reaches Top 3 Tinashe Sends Message to Republican Fans During 2026 Midterm Elections: 'Y'all Can Go'

Launched by YG Entertainment, BIGBANG arrived in August 2006 and broke new ground for the Korean-pop scene by winning the inaugural best worldwide act trophy onstage at the 2011 MTV Europe Music Awards. What the Democratic midterms sweep changes for U.S.-China relations

📅 2026-11-05  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

BEIJING — Democrats taking power from Republicans in Congress raises risks for U.S.-China tensions around Taiwan, while hardening tech competition, analysts said.

The Democrats swept this week's midterm elections, taking majorities in the House and Senate and flipping governors' offices in places like Texas, Nevada and Iowa. Most of the new election winners, however, won't begin their terms until January.

Before then, U.S. President Donald Trump and Chinese President Xi Jinping will likely maintain their fragile truce by meeting twice: in Shenzhen this month, and in Miami, Florida, next month.

"As a lame duck who is increasingly focused on his personal legacy rather than the Republican Party's success, Trump may resist increased pressure for confrontation with China and continue his pursuit of 'strategic stability,'" Gabriel Wildau, managing director at Teneo, said in an email.

High on the Democrats' agenda is removing Trump's tariffs, as part of a broader focus on addressing domestic inflation, CNBC has learned.

"On balance as a result of the midterms the Democrats may nudge the U.S.'s China policy in a more hawkish direction through hearings and legislation, particularly on economic security issues and Taiwan," said Scott Kennedy, senior advisor and trustee chair in Chinese business and economics at the Center for Strategic and International Studies.

But any efforts will "be constrained by the executive branch's wide range of latitude and China's growing power and leverage, which can't be easily undone," he said.

Beijing has hosted a flurry of global leaders this year, while major economies have struggled to slow a surge of imports from China. Chinese dominance in rare earths has given Beijing leverage in trade talks with the U.S. and the EU.

"Taiwan remains the most dangerous issue in U.S.-China relations and, in my view, the area where a Democratic Congress is most likely to challenge Beijing," wrote George Chen, partner and co-chair of digital practice at consultancy The Asia Group.

"Congress has repeatedly shown a willingness to pursue Taiwan initiatives even when administrations are more cautious," he said, noting Trump's balancing act will likely become more difficult.

Nancy Pelosi's visit to Taiwan while House Speaker in 2022 angered Beijing, which considers the democratically self-governed island part of its territory. The U.S. maintains unofficial relations with Taiwan under a "One China" policy.

Minority Leader Hakeem Jeffries (D-N.Y.), who is poised to become the first Black Speaker of the House in U.S. history, met in 2023 in New York with Tsai Ing-wen, who was then president of Taiwan.

During Trump's trip to Beijing in May, Xi warned that mishandling Taiwan would put U.S.-China relations in "great jeopardy."

Maintaining stability over Taiwan has grown more critical in the artificial intelligence race as the island is home to semiconductor giant TSMC, which manufactures many of Nvidia's chips.

Over the last several years, the U.S. government has tightened restrictions on the ability of Chinese companies to buy advanced chips from Nvidia, spurring Beijing's efforts to develop its own version of the technology.

U.S. stocks surged Wednesday after Trump accepted the Democrats' election wins, and oil prices fell following indications Washington would do more to end the Iran war. Apple and most "Magnificent Seven" tech stocks surged, despite declines in Nvidia.

But there's an "emergence of a broader consensus in Washington that America's technological lead should not be used to strengthen China's military or strategic capabilities," Chen said, noting a Democratic Congress may be motivated to be more aggressive.

"As a result, Beijing may discover that technology becomes a growing source of friction even if Trump and Xi continue to maintain relatively cordial summit diplomacy," he said.

The tech outlook ultimately depends on bigger questions about AI.

Chinese tech stocks climbed Thursday, extending a recent rebound from a sharp sell-off last month.

Beijing's latest regulatory intervention has helped support the market, said WisdomTree's Liqian Ren. But she said Chinese tech stock performance is ultimately tied more to global investor sentiment on AI.

"There's a lot of uncertainty on how much AI can deliver for U.S., or for China," she said.

—CNBC's Justin Papp and Kevin Breuninger contributed to this report.
